## rexglue-temporal-aa-shaders-vk.patch

**Why.** FSR's Vulkan runtime embeds its shaders as SPIR-V in headers that FidelityFX's shader compiler, FidelityFX_SC.exe, generates at build time; it is a Windows program, so Linux and the PS5 (whose build host is Linux) can't run it. This patch adds those headers, prebuilt, for the permutations rexglue's native anti-aliasing can ask for, so neither needs Wine or a Windows tool. Generated, so in a patch of its own; `rexglue-temporal-aa.patch` uses it.

**What it adds.** `thirdparty/fidelityfx-shaders-vk/`, made by `launcher/dev/fsr/fsr_vk_shaders.py` from a Windows build's FidelityFX_SC output at the pinned FidelityFX commit:
- For each of the FSR 3 upscaler's ten passes and its four device variants (wave32 or 64, FP16 or not; the device picks), the permutation table (`*_permutations.h`) with the keys rexglue's create flags reach (HDR input, inverted depth, vectors at the render size, no jitter cancellation: four of 64, the Lanczos table that the device picks times sharpening) leading to their blobs, unchanged, and every other key to an empty entry; tables FidelityFX's code doesn't use (the 16-bit ones of the passes without FP16, whose blobs its runtime doesn't embed) empty.
- The same for frame generation (`rexglue-frame-generation.patch`): frame interpolation's 11 passes and optical flow's 7, for the create flags of `frame_generation_fsr.h` (`REXGLUE_FSR_SHADERS_VK_FG_FLAGS`): 40 more table entries (26 and 14).
- Each blob is stored once: FidelityFX_SC writes the same SPIR-V for several variants of a pass (wave64, 16-bit), and those tables point at one header. 38 distinct blobs, 0.72 MB of SPIR-V, 4.28 MB as headers (185 files); before frame generation and the folding the set was 40 blobs, 0.94 MB and 5.17 MB.
- FSR 2's 32 tables empty (ffx-api links FSR 2, but rexglue asks for FSR 3.1).
- `rexglue_fsr_shaders_vk.h`: the create flags the set is for, of those that choose FSR 3's permutations (and frame generation's); the GPU plugin compares its own with them before it creates FSR from this set.
- FidelityFX's MIT licence and a README.

The script stops without writing on a FidelityFX checkout that isn't the pinned commit or has other trees or modified files (as `rexglue_fsr.cmake` checks it), on an option it doesn't know, on create flags in the GPU plugin it can't map to options (`kTaaFsrCreateFlags` in `temporal_aa_fsr.h`, which both backends must use as they are), on FidelityFX choosing its permutations from the flags differently from what it assumes (the lines of `ffx_fsr3upscaler.cpp` it checks), on a table the accessor indexes with a key of another layout, on a table whose blobs the runtime embeds only in part, on a build that itself used the prebuilt set, and when its own output has a key rexglue can ask for that leads to an empty entry. Its output doesn't depend on the order FidelityFX_SC writes things in (it varies from run to run). Run it again after a change of the FidelityFX commit or of the create flags, and remake the patch.

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| The prebuilt shaders, their flags, licence and README | `thirdparty/fidelityfx-shaders-vk/` | Linux, the PS5 (Windows only with `REXGLUE_FSR_PREBUILT_SHADERS=ON`) |

## rexglue-fullscreen-setting-ui-thread.patch

**Why.** The SDK applies a change of `fullscreen` in an option change callback, and those run on whatever thread sets the option, inside `SetFlagByName`, with the option registry's lock held. The game's pause menu sets it from the game's thread (FULLSCREEN in DISPLAY), as the dev pad does from its own. Changing the window from there sends window messages to the UI thread and waits for them (SDL's `SetWindowLongW`). While the F4 settings overlay or the console overlay is open, the UI thread reads options as it draws (`SettingsDialog::OnDraw`, `cvar::Query`), so it waited for the lock the setting thread held, and the game hung: the GPU thread stopped too, in its per-frame `GetFlagByName`. Found with a stack dump of the hung game (2026-10-17). Upstream's SDK has the same callback (`src/ui/rex_app.cpp`, v0.10.0).

**What it changes.** The callback hands the change to the UI thread (`CallInUIThreadDeferred`), which makes it after the lock is released, as the game's `mcla_hide_cursor` callback already does. Changes are applied in the order they were made, and SDL's window functions are now called on the thread they belong to. The other option callbacks (`log_level`, `hdr`, `hdr_sdr_eotf`, `mcla_display_gamma`, `mcla_hide_cursor`) only store values or post to the UI thread, so they stay as they are; running every callback after the lock is released would let two threads' changes to one option apply out of order.

**Checked** with `launcher/dev/scripts/display-switches.pad` (Windows, RTX 5080): HDR off and on, then fullscreen on and off, with no overlay, with the F4 settings overlay open, and with the console overlay open. It passed on D3D12, on Vulkan (IMMEDIATE) and on Vulkan with FIFO_RELAXED, where the UI thread paints every frame: every run ended by itself, swaps kept rising at every step, and every fullscreen switch resized the swap chain. Before the patch the same switch with the F4 overlay open hung D3D12 every time. With no overlay open it worked before as well.

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| `fullscreen` applied on the UI thread | `src/ui/rex_app.cpp` | all platforms with a window |

## rexglue-presenter-ui-overlay.patch

**Why.** While any UI drawer is registered (an ImGui dialog such as the F4 settings overlay or the console, the achievement pop-up), the SDK's presenter paints from the UI thread. It presents at the display's rate and shows each guest frame when it gets to it, on the queue the GPU command thread submits to. With the F4 overlay or the console open the game's frame rate fell by half or more (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Frame generation needs every guest frame painted by the guest output thread, in the queue right after the frame's own work. This patch adds that way of painting with the UI still shown, UI overlay painting, and makes it the default.

**What it adds.**
- **`present_ui_overlay`** (on; restart). Frame generation turns it on too.
  - **Where it applies:** where the guest output thread would present without UI too, so not with implicit vertical sync and not with `host_present_from_non_ui_thread` off. On Vulkan that means only with the IMMEDIATE or MAILBOX present mode; with FIFO alone the presenter paints as before. Frame generation forces it everywhere, since its pacer presents.
  - **Not checked on the PS5 or Linux.** Both keep the previous way of painting where their swap chain offers only FIFO.
  - **The UI's refresh rate:** the UI is shown with each guest frame, so it refreshes at the game's frame rate (30 a second at the launcher's default cap) instead of the display's. That is how a game's own menus behave; the mouse pointer, which Windows draws, stays smooth.
  - **The guest output thread** paints every guest frame, drawers or not.
  - **The UI thread** no longer presents. It keeps the connection (reconnecting when a paint finds it outdated, without taking the paint lock to find out). It draws the drawers into one of two overlay layers (8-bit, premultiplied, the swap chain's size) on a command list of its own, at its usual tick rate and again for each guest frame. The second is what the dialogs that ask for no repaints of their own (the achievement pop-up) rely on, as they do without the overlay.
  - **The layers** are made when the first drawer is shown, so a session with no presenter UI never allocates them.
  - **The composite:** the guest output thread's paint samples the newest finished layer where it is and puts it over the image, with `ui_overlay_composite.ps` in SDR (one-to-one, premultiplied blending) and `hdr_ui_composite.ps` over HDR10, with the UI levels the HDR UI layer uses.
    - **Ordering:** the UI thread's drawing and the paints are on the same queue. On D3D12 the layer's transitions order them; on Vulkan, the layer render pass's dependencies.
    - **Nothing over the image** once the last drawer is gone, which covers a dialog that closed itself while being drawn.
- **Fallbacks.**
  - **No guest frame for 250 ms while a drawer is registered** (1 s with frame generation, whose pacer then stops presenting), also before the first guest frame: the UI thread paints and presents as before.
    - **Resuming:** only once guest frames have come for 250 ms with no gap over 100 ms, so that a loading hitch can't switch it back and forth.
    - **The switch back:** before the guest output thread paints again, the UI thread draws the overlay, then shows the latest guest frame with it if it hasn't been shown. That paint goes through the pacer with frame generation, and is thread r in the present log. So no frame goes without the UI or with the overlay from before the suspension.
    - **Logs:** both switches are logged. `present_debug_stall_ms` makes the guest output thread sleep once, to test them.
  - **The overlay can't be drawn:** the UI thread paints as before until the next connection, logged.
- **The present log** (`present_log=<csv>`, development):
  - one line per guest output refresh: the steady clock in ns, the refresh's number, 0 if not published;
  - one line per paint: the refresh it showed, the painting thread, the result, its duration, and the UI frame it showed (numbered by the UI thread whenever it draws the drawers, 0 for none);
  - one line per suspension or resumption;
  - flushed about once a second.

  `launcher/dev/presents.py` lines it up with the swaps of `frames.csv` and PresentMon's capture (`devrun.py --present-log --presentmon`).
  - It counts the paints with UI per segment.
  - Around each suspension and resumption it checks for paints without UI, paints with an older UI frame than one already shown, and guest frames shown twice after a resumption.
  - An implementation's pacer thread logs its presents as thread q (`LogPresentFromPacer`), with result -1 for a frame it dropped.
- **Checked** on Windows (RTX 5080, 1920x1080 window, 240 Hz display, PresentMon and the present log).
  - **As the default:** on and off, `launcher/dev/fg/ui-overlay-eval.pad`, both APIs, native and 3×, HDR and SDR, with F4, the achievement pop-up and the console. With it on, every segment had presents per swap 1.0000 (one 1.0007), no guest frame never shown or shown twice, and no UI-thread paint.

    | Game frames a second, F4 / console open | On | Off (presents per swap) |
    |---|---|---|
    | D3D12 native, HDR / SDR | 146 / 146, 146 / 146 | 76 / 66 (x3.2 / x3.6), 77 / 60 (x3.1 / x4.0) |
    | Vulkan native, HDR / SDR | 146 / 146, 146 / 146 | 60 / 101 (x4.0 / x2.4), 61 / 113 (x3.9 / x2.1) |
    | D3D12 3×, HDR / SDR | 135 / 134, 142 / 145 | 134 / 135 (x1.5 / x1.5), 85 / 69 (x2.8 / x3.4) |
    | Vulkan 3×, HDR / SDR | 122 / 121, 127 / 125 | 124 / 122 (x1.5 / x1.5), 54 / 130 (x4.4 / x1.5; with the console 45 frames never shown) |

    The Vulkan 3× row is with `rexglue-vulkan-paint-pipeline-format.patch`. Before it, off showed only half the guest frames with any drawer, the pop-up too, and the GPU thread's paint took 4.2 ms at 3× (now 0.08 ms in HDR, 0.16 ms in SDR, and frame generation's paint for the pacer 0.06 ms).

    - **No UI:** the same as off (40 s drive, native HDR): presents per swap 1.0000 on both, paint 0.20 / 0.21 ms (D3D12) and 0.17 / 0.17 ms (Vulkan), GPU 3.72 / 3.76 and 3.93 / 3.99 ms per frame.
    - **The overlay's GPU cost:** 0.03 to 0.10 ms per game frame at 1080p, with a drawer open against just after it closed, where the GPU is the limit (3×).
    - **At a 30 FPS cap:** the game held 30 either way. With F4 or the console open, off presented 8 times per game frame and kept the GPU 17 to 20% busy, against 12 to 13% on.
    - **Shown correctly:** F4, the pop-up, the console and a dialog that closes itself from its own draw, captured on both APIs in HDR and SDR, the dialog gone after it closed with nothing left behind.
  - **The suspension and resumption,** `launcher/dev/fg/ui-overlay-suspension.pad`, both APIs:
    - with the console open from the start, before the first guest frame;
    - with F4 open, a 400 ms hitch, four 300 ms hitches 150 ms apart (one suspension and one resumption, no switching back and forth), and a 3 s stall;
    - the pause menu with F4 and the pop-up (no suspension: the game keeps drawing).

    Around every switch the present log showed no paint without UI, none with an older UI frame and no guest frame shown twice after a resumption. In the 250 ms of steady frames before a resumption the UI thread paints as before, so some guest frames are shown twice there (15 to 20 after one hitch or stall, 42 to 51 over the series of four), none lost.
  - **F4 open over the career's load into the city and a race's load,** `launcher/dev/fg/ui-overlay-loads.pad`, both APIs: no suspension (the game keeps drawing its loading screens). Every guest frame was presented once, with the UI.
  - **With frame generation's pacer:** `launcher/dev/fg/fg-mode.pad`, below.

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| UI overlay painting, its fallbacks, the present log | `include/rex/ui/presenter.h`, `src/ui/presenter.cpp` | all platforms |
| The overlay layers and their composite | `include/rex/ui/d3d12/d3d12_presenter.h`, `src/ui/d3d12/d3d12_presenter.cpp`, `include/rex/ui/vulkan/presenter.h`, `src/ui/vulkan/vulkan_presenter.cpp`, `src/ui/vulkan/vulkan_presenter_ui_overlay.cpp`, `src/ui/CMakeLists.txt` | D3D12; Vulkan (all platforms) |
| The overlay composite shader, compiled | `src/ui/shaders/ui_overlay_composite.ps.xesl`, `src/ui/shaders/bytecode/d3d12_5_1/ui_overlay_composite_ps.h`, `src/ui/shaders/vulkan_spirv/ui_overlay_composite_ps.h` | D3D12; Vulkan |

## rexglue-frame-generation.patch

**Why.** AMD FSR 3.1 frame generation, an interpolated frame between every two the game renders. In development, in phases:
- **Phase 0:** the build of FidelityFX's frame generation and the way frames are presented for it.
- **Phase 1:** generation on Direct3D 12 with HDR output and FSR anti-aliasing.
- **Phase 2:** generation on Direct3D 12 in SDR too, and with MSAA or anti-aliasing off.
- **Phase 3:** generation on Vulkan, with the same options, shown by the presenter's own frame pacer.
- **Phase 4 (this):** the settings: frame generation switches on and off while playing on both graphics APIs, its state is a pair of read-only settings the pause menu reads, and it has a cap of its own, HALF DISPLAY by default (below). The game's pause menu (DISPLAY > FRAME GENERATION, and FPS TARGET with HALF DISPLAY) and the launcher use them (`README.md`, `launcher/README.md`).
- **Since then:** low latency (`rexglue-low-latency.patch`), and Linux and the PS5 (`rexglue-frame-generation-nonwindows.patch`: the frame generation components in their FidelityFX runtime, the device's runtime check, the PS5's pacing).

Windows is the only platform it has run on. Linux and the PS5 offer it, untested (`rexglue-frame-generation-nonwindows.patch` is what they got): the Vulkan code is the same there, built but not run, and the PS5's device gets FSR's features only when the game starts with `frame_generation` (or FSR anti-aliasing) chosen, so choosing it during play takes effect at the next start there (the menu says NEXT START).

**What it adds.**
- **`frame_generation`** (off or fsr; changes while playing; Windows, Linux and the PS5 untested). fsr turns on UI overlay painting (`rexglue-presenter-ui-overlay.patch`). On Direct3D 12 it generates frames through AMD's swap chain (below); on Vulkan the command processor generates them and the presenter's frame pacer shows them (below).
- **Switching while playing** (phase 4).
  - **How:** the presenter follows the setting (a change callback; each change is logged), and makes the next connection as it asks. Vulkan makes its swap chain again with the present mode and the pacer the new state needs (the connection check that already compared `swapchain_frame_generation_wanted`); Direct3D 12 makes the swap chain again at the next paint when the one it has (AMD's frame interpolation swap chain or a plain DXGI one) isn't the one the state needs, in either direction. UI overlay painting is available from the start wherever generation can run (only painted when `present_ui_overlay` or generation is on), so that it can be switched on later. On Windows the row needs no NEXT START on either API (the PS5's device is the exception, below).
  - **The cost** (the largest gap between two presents within 1.5 s of a switch, 240 Hz display, 1280x720): Direct3D 12 turning it on 53.5 ms the first time and 33.1 ms the second, turning it off 17.4 and 17.0 ms; Vulkan turning it on 20.1 and 9.3 ms, off 15.5 and 9.4 ms (steady 8.3 ms at 120 swaps a second). The first Direct3D 12 switch is the longest: FidelityFX's runtime and AMD's swap chain are made then (the context's own set-up is logged at 23 ms); generation's GPU context is made at the first frame that has inputs.
  - **The Vulkan device** makes the pacer's second queue and the timeline semaphores wherever generation can run (checked when it is made), not only with the setting on, so the setting can be turned on later; the queue is the only cost with it off (swaps per second uncapped, native, 1920x1080, 40 s: 142.90 against 142.91 before on Vulkan, 142.95 against 142.84 on Direct3D 12; the game's mean GPU use 50 and 48% against 47 and 51%, run to run noise).
  - **Its state for settings screens:** `Presenter::GetFrameGenerationStatus()` (any thread): unknown before the presenter starts, available, or unavailable with a reason in the menu's letters (A to Z, 0 to 9, space, `+ . ( )`). It is a value of the presenter's (a mutex-guarded struct, nothing calls back from it), not a cvar: a cvar set from code is a runtime value, which the F4 overlay's Save (`rex::cvar::SaveConfig`) writes into `mcla.toml`, and a file or the command line could set it. The reasons: `NEEDS VULKAN 1.3`, `NEXT START` (the device was made without FSR's features: only on the PS5, where they come with the setting at the start), `NO TIMELINE SEMAPHORES`, `NO FIDELITYFX RUNTIME` (`amd_fidelityfx_vk.dll` or `amd_fidelityfx_dx12.dll` isn't next to the executable), `NOT BUILT`, `NOT AVAILABLE HERE` (a graphics backend without it), and `FAILED` (found while running, below). `Presenter::GetDisplayRefreshHz()` is the refresh rate of the display the window is on (the display state worker reads it with HDR's: `EnumDisplaySettingsW`; a variable-refresh display's highest; 0 until read or where it isn't), logged with the display's HDR state.
- **Direct3D 12 generation** (`src/ui/d3d12/d3d12_presenter_frame_generation.cpp`, `src/graphics/d3d12/frame_generation.cpp`).
  - **AMD's frame interpolation swap chain** (ffx-api's, from `amd_fidelityfx_dx12.dll` next to the executable) replaces DXGI's when the connection is made.
    - It presents on a queue of its own, half a frame apart, with its pacing (hybrid spin on, its fence waited with `WaitForSingleObject` when far off).
    - Its `Present` for a frame waits until the previous frame's presents are composed: the guest output thread never runs more than a frame ahead, and no frame is dropped.
    - Everything it presents is composed by the presenter's callback, from the presenter's images; its own back buffers stay unused while generating.
  - **The inputs**, given by the refresher with each guest frame:
    - the HUD-less image: `hdr_refresh_fg.cs`, the HDR refresher with one more output, the scene's code values before the UI, as FP16. In HDR they're times 0.75 with a shoulder above 0.8 (optical flow keeps an 8-bit luma); in SDR they're as they are, and the SDR refresher still writes the frame itself, `hdr_refresh_fg.cs` then writing only the HUD-less image;
    - the depth, copied at the anti-aliasing draw (the UI may resolve into its memory before the swap);
    - the motion vectors, jitter, camera and cut detection of temporal anti-aliasing's pass (`rexglue-temporal-aa.patch`). Without FSR anti-aliasing (MSAA or off) the pass runs for the vectors alone: the anti-aliasing draw is found and its camera taken, nothing is jittered, no FSR context is made.
  - **The UI separated in SDR too** (`rexglue-hdr-ui-layer.patch`'s, otherwise HDR-only) while AMD's swap chain is in use: about 0.2 ms a frame at 3x (M2's figures). The HDR scene pass stays HDR-only.
  - **Unavailable:** where FidelityFX's Direct3D 12 runtime can't be loaded, AMD's swap chain or the context can't be made, or a generation fails on the GPU's queue, the presenter gives the command processor no inputs (the depth copy, the vector pass, the HUD-less writes and the SDR UI separation stop), says so once, and the next connection makes a plain DXGI swap chain (UI overlay painting isn't forced). It lasts until the next start; `frame_generation_debug_fail` (development: 1 the context can't be created, 2 the generation fails) forces it.
  - **Decided once:** the presenter decides at start-up and takes the inputs at each swap; the depth copy and the vectors-only pass run only while it does (a `frame_generation` changed while running has no effect until the next start).
  - **Per frame**, on the guest output thread right after the frame's work: the presenter's overlay is copied, generation is prepared, `ffxConfigure` takes the frame's ID and the callbacks, and the swap chain's `Present` calls the generation callback on that thread.
  - **The generation callback:** FSR 3.1 frame interpolation from the previous HUD-less image to this one, then the generated frame's compose: `hdr_refresh_fg.cs` again, from the generated code values, with this frame's UI layer and levels (in SDR, the display table at the nearest code, as the SDR refresher writes a frame). Both on AMD's command list for the game queue.
  - **The generation size:** the guest output's, or, where the frame is shown smaller (a window smaller than the output), exactly the size it's shown at. The HUD-less image and the UI layer are scaled to it bilinearly at the presenter's sample positions, the generated frame is presented 1:1, and the real one is scaled by the presenter to the same size, so both are scaled alike at any ratio.
  - **The present callback:** real frames exactly as without generation (the refresher's output, scaled bilinearly), generated frames from their compose, the presenter's overlay over both.
  - **Not generated** (the real frame shown in the generated one's place, and the next generation starts afresh):
    - frames without the game's 3D scene (no tone map: menus drawn alone, logos, videos);
    - the UI separation's fallbacks (the difference mask, the whole frame);
    - the HDR debug views and the test pattern;
    - frames without motion vectors (no anti-aliasing draw that frame);
    - in SDR, with FXAA or the game's piecewise linear ramp (the generated frame's compose has neither);
    - the UI thread's paints (while the guest output thread is silent, generation is off on AMD's swap chain, and its presents copy the back buffer).
  - **Pacing:** `frame_generation_pacing` (`auto`, `timed`, `display`; below, with the platforms' patch): `timed` is the pacer described here; `display` presents the two frames back to back and lets a fixed-refresh display space them (the PS5's, untested on the console).
  - **Presents:** tear-free by default. AMD's swap chain presents with SyncInterval 1 and no flags then (logged once); `frame_generation_present=tearing` (read at connection) gives SyncInterval 0 and `DXGI_PRESENT_ALLOW_TEARING`.
    - **Tear-free, verified with G-SYNC** (a 240 Hz display) in a window and in borderless fullscreen, both independent flip: at the display's rate every present is shown for exactly one refresh (4.17 ms), and below it for a time that varies with the frames (variable refresh rate). PresentMon reports these presents as SyncInterval 0 with tearing allowed, and it reports a plain DXGI `Present(1, 0)` (frame generation off) the same way on this display: that's how it shows vertical sync under G-SYNC in independent flip, not a driver override (the NVIDIA profile, read without changing anything, has none). A display with a fixed refresh rate is untested.
  - **After 1200 frames in a row with nothing to generate from,** the frame generation context (hundreds of MB at 4K) is released until there is.
  - **Memory with frame generation on:** AMD's swap chain keeps 3 replacement and 2 interpolation buffers at the swap chain's size (about 165 MB at 4K HDR10), used only while generation is off.
  - **AMD's `Present` waits without a time limit** (for the previous frame's composition, its interpolation and a free buffer) while the guest output thread holds the paint lock. A hang in AMD's presenter thread or in DXGI would stop the UI thread with it. That's AMD's code; it isn't bounded here.
  - **Async compute (not offered):** AMD's `allowAsyncWorkloads` (with `FFX_FRAMEGENERATION_ENABLE_ASYNC_WORKLOAD_SUPPORT` at the context's creation) was tried on Direct3D 12 behind a dev cvar and taken out: the callbacks then record on a compute command list, and our compose pass is a pixel-shader pass whose barriers (`PIXEL_SHADER_RESOURCE`) are invalid there (debug layer: `RESOURCE_BARRIER_INVALID_COMMAND_LIST_TYPE`, then the device is removed). Making the compose a compute pass is what it would take; the cost it could hide is 0.39 ms a generated frame at 1280x720 (about 2.3 ms at 4K, 3x). Vulkan runs generation in the command processor's own command buffer and has no async path. Off, as designed; nothing measured.
  - **Development:**
    - `frame_generation_dump=N` writes the next N frames' real and generated images (HDR10 or SDR codes; the real one at the guest output's size, the generated one at the generation size), and `launcher/dev/fg/fgcheck.py` checks them, scaling the real frames as the presenter does;
    - `frame_generation_debug`: 1 FidelityFX's tear lines, 2 its debug view, 3 only the generated frames presented;
    - `frame_generation_timing` logs the GPU time of generation and its compose (timestamps around them).
- **FidelityFX runtimes:**
  - **The provider:** the frame generation provider in the ffx-api runtimes (`FFX_API_ENABLE_FRAMEGEN_PROVIDER`), with frame interpolation and optical flow. Linux and the PS5 build it too since `rexglue-frame-generation-nonwindows.patch` (`REXGLUE_FSR_FRAMEGEN=OFF` leaves it out of the runtime).
  - **The Vulkan stub:** on Vulkan the frame interpolation swapchain is replaced by a stub (`cmake/fidelityfx/ffx_provider_framegenerationswapchain_vk_stub.cpp`). rexglue drives frame generation itself, and AMD's Vulkan swapchain at this commit doesn't build with Clang.
  - **Build safeguards:** a configure check stops the build if the Vulkan backend ever gets the provider's define. The wrapper's files are hashed into the runtime's configure, so changing one rebuilds it.
  - **Headers:** `ffx_framegeneration.h` (MIT, unmodified), and `src/graphics/frame_generation_fsr.h` with the create flags. A `static_assert` checks those flags against the prebuilt Vulkan set's (`rexglue-temporal-aa-shaders-vk.patch`).
- **Vulkan generation** (`src/graphics/vulkan/frame_generation.cpp`, the refresher in `src/graphics/vulkan/command_processor.cpp`, the vectors in `src/graphics/vulkan/temporal_aa.cpp`; the presenter's part is the pacer, below).
  - **FidelityFX's context is the command processor's,** from the same Vulkan runtime as FSR's (`amd_fidelityfx_vk`, through FSR's KHR alias wrapper, and the PS5's static path) and in manual mode (`FFX_FRAMEGENERATION_FLAG_NO_SWAPCHAIN_CONTEXT_NOTIFY`): AMD's Vulkan swap chain isn't used. Configuring, preparing and dispatching are an external callback in the swap's own command buffer on the submission thread, so generation runs after frame N's work and before frame N+1's, and the generated frame's compose follows it in the same submission. The context is made on another thread at the first frame that can be generated (tens of milliseconds with the prebuilt shaders) and released after 1200 frames in a row with nothing to generate from, as on Direct3D 12.
  - **One state says whether generation can run,** and everything that exists for it follows: the inputs, the depth copy, the vector pass, the HUD-less writes, the SDR UI separation, and the pacer with its FIFO mode and back-pressure. It is false where the device can't (checked when it's made: Vulkan 1.3 with FSR's features, the timeline semaphores, and `amd_fidelityfx_vk.dll` next to the executable; the device then gets no second queue, the presenter no pacer, and the log says why; Direct3D 12 checks `amd_fidelityfx_dx12.dll`), and, found while running, where the pipelines or FidelityFX's context can't be made, temporal anti-aliasing's pass (the vectors) isn't available, or a generation fails on the GPU's queue. The command processor tells the presenter (the inputs' `unavailable_reason`), which says so once and makes the next connection as without frame generation: the usual present modes and queue, no pacer, UI overlay painting as `present_ui_overlay` has it. It lasts until the next start; `frame_generation_debug_fail` (development: 1 the context can't be created, 2 the generation fails) forces it. Nothing of Vulkan's generation is made with frame generation off (its pipelines come at the first frame with inputs); Direct3D 12's two small compute pipelines are made at start-up.
  - **The inputs are Direct3D 12's:**
    - the HUD-less image: `hdr_refresh_fg.cs` (in HDR the refresher's own dispatch with a fifth descriptor set and flag bit 13; in SDR one more dispatch right after the SDR refresher's, flag bits 13 to 15, with a barrier between them as the shader can write the first one's output), scaled to the generation size by `fg_hudless_downscale.cs` where that is smaller;
    - the depth, copied at the anti-aliasing draw by `fg_depth_copy.cs` (the guest's 2D-array depth texture into an R32F image: it can't be a transfer source, and the UI may resolve into its memory before the swap);
    - the motion vectors, jitter, camera and cut detection of temporal anti-aliasing's pass; with MSAA or anti-aliasing off the pass runs for the vectors alone (`kVectorsOnly`, no FSR context), as on Direct3D 12.
  - **Not generated** (the real frame alone, and the next generation starts afresh, also for the frames the presenter gave no inputs for while it reconnects): the same cases as on Direct3D 12 (no tone map, the UI separation's fallbacks, the HDR debug views, no motion vectors, FXAA or the piecewise linear ramp in SDR), and a frame whose generation failed on the submission thread (its generated frame isn't shown, and generation becomes unavailable, above). After 1200 frames in a row without generation (also those without inputs: a minimised window) the context and the images are released.
  - **The generation size** is Direct3D 12's rule too: the guest output's, or exactly the size the frame is shown at where that is smaller (a window smaller than the output). The presenter gives the refresher the image to compose the generated frame into (`FrameGenerationInputs`), at that size, and takes it back like the guest output image.
  - **The compose** is `hdr_refresh_fg.cs` again, from the generated code values, with this frame's UI layer and levels (in SDR the display table at the nearest code), written into that image; the presenter paints it into the pair's first present image as it paints the real frame (bilinear, the overlay over it).
  - **FidelityFX copies the HUD-less image** (into its history) with transfers: the generation images have transfer usage, which synchronization validation found missing.
  - **Development:** `frame_generation_timing` (now defined in the presenter, read on both APIs) logs the GPU time of the preparation, generation and compose, every 300 generated frames; `frame_generation_dump=N` and `launcher/dev/fg/fgcheck.py` as on Direct3D 12 (`fg_dump_vulkan_*`: the presented images, at the window's size, `real` and `generated`, or `real_only`); `frame_generation_debug` 1, 2 and 3 as there (3 presents the generated frames only).
- **Vulkan device.** Wherever generation can run (above), whether `frame_generation` asks for it now or not, the device gets a second queue for the pacer: queue 1 of the graphics family where it has two, as NVIDIA's does, otherwise one of another family that can present. `frame_generation_debug_pacer_queue` (development: auto, other_family, none) forces the choice, to test the other paths. The queue gets no global priority yet.
- **The Vulkan frame pacer.**
  - **Paint and present:** the guest output thread paints each frame into a pair of present images (the generated frame, then the real one, in one of two slots; the swap chain's format and size, shared by both queue families) and hands the pair to the pacer thread. That thread, for each image of the pair, acquires a swap chain image with a fence it waits for on the CPU, copies the image into it and presents it on its own queue. A frame with no generated frame (not generated, or generation still being made) is just the real one. A present waiting for vertical sync never holds the graphics queue or its lock: the pacer never shares that queue.
  - **Ordering:** timeline semaphores order paint, copy and the next paint into the same images.
  - **Pacing** (AMD's formulas, MIT, as its Direct3D 12 swap chain applies them): the presents are half the rendered interval apart, less a tenth of its mean deviation and 0.1 ms. The generated frame goes as soon as it's ready but not before the last present plus that, the real one that after it; the pacer thread (highest priority, as AMD's presenter threads are, with its own high-resolution waitable timer, so it doesn't depend on the game having raised the system's timer resolution) sleeps to 0.5 ms before the target and spins the rest (1.5 ms with `timeBeginPeriod(1)` for its life where the timer is missing, before Windows 10 1803). The rendered interval is the time between the paints' completions as the pacer sees them, the last ten (a pause of over half a second, or a new connection, starts the window again; a shorter hitch stays in it). Where a paint was already done when the pacer got to it, the pacer's own presents set the pace, and pacing from that kept the rate wherever a slow moment had left it with `frame_generation_present=tearing` (100 rendered a second instead of 120, with nothing else limiting): the interval taken is then 10% under the window's mean, so the gaps shrink until the pacer no longer holds the game back. (The guest output thread's interval between its pushes, less its waits for the pacer, was tried: it counted the pacer's waits for the GPU too, and at 3x it put the generated and real frames' presents 2.4 and 8.7 ms apart.)
  - **Present mode:** FIFO, tear-free (with variable refresh rate where the display has it), or IMMEDIATE with `frame_generation_present=tearing`, read at connection; MAILBOX never, as it could replace a generated frame with the real one before it's shown.
  - **Back-pressure:** the guest output thread waits until the pacer has taken the previous frame, before it takes the paint lock: up to a second, and then (the pacer is stalled) it drops its frames without waiting until the pacer takes one, instead of a second for each; a slot's wait under the paint lock is 100 ms. The pacer's own waits are cut into slices that see a request to stop and a device loss. So a present or acquire blocked in the driver doesn't stop the guest output thread (the game goes on, dropping frames), and the UI thread's overlay paints go on.
  - **Reconnects:** a resize or HDR switch pauses the pacer and drops the frames it hadn't taken. Every dropped frame is counted per connection and appears in the present log; the log lines are the first 20 of a connection and every 100th after. The UI thread waits at a reconnect for a present stuck in the driver (logging every second): the pacer's objects can't go before it's done, and retiring the swapchain waits on that queue anyway.
  - **No pacer for a connection** (no queue of its own that can present to the surface, or a swap chain that can't be copied into): it paints as without frame generation, logged. UI overlay painting isn't forced, so implicit vsync stays with the UI thread, and MAILBOX stays allowed. There is no colour-attachment fallback for such swap chains yet.
  - **`frame_generation_dump=N`** (development) writes the next N presented images as PPM files.
  - **Limits:**
    - while UI overlay painting is suspended (a guest frame silent for 1 s with a drawer open) the refresher still generates frames that nobody paints with a pair;
    - the HDR metadata is set on the guest output thread while the pacer may be presenting (a window of microseconds when the peak setting or the display changes; DXGI serialises it on NVIDIA);
    - `frame_generation_present=tearing` falls back to FIFO, logged, where the surface has no IMMEDIATE mode;
    - with a non-bilinear `present_effect` the pacer's real frames are scaled bilinearly too (as the generated ones are, and as HDR always does), so both look alike;
    - the pacer's queue has no global priority yet, and on a GPU whose graphics family has one queue and no other family that can present there is no frame generation (no shared-queue fallback).
- **Checked, phase 1** (Direct3D 12, HDR, FSR anti-aliasing; Windows, RTX 5080, 1920x1080 window, 240 Hz display, PresentMon and the present log).
  - **Every guest frame presented, with one generated frame each:**
    - native: 120 frames rendered and 240 shown a second (the display's rate), presents per swap 2.0000, every real frame shown once, between presents 4.17 ms (p1 3.98, p99 4.35), all displayed;
    - the race benchmark (`race.pad`): presents per swap 2.0000 through the menus, the race's loading and intro and the race (119 rendered, 238 shown);
    - with F4, the achievement pop-up and the console open (`launcher/dev/fg/ui-overlay-eval.pad`): the same, and the overlay over every frame (captured).
  - **3x in the 1920x1080 window** (GPU-bound, 40 s drive), generated at 1920x1080:

    | | Rendered a second | Shown | Frame time (1000 / rendered) |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | Without generation | 118 | 118 | 8.48 ms |
    | Generated at the guest output's size (3840x2160) | 83 | 166 | 12.02 ms |
    | Generated at the window's size | 91 | 183 | 10.93 ms |

    The frame-time difference (2.45 ms) includes the wait in AMD's `Present`. The GPU time of generation and its compose (`frame_generation_timing`, means of 300) is 0.85 to 1.6 ms per generated frame at 2561x1440 (varying with the scene), and 1.7 to 2.5 ms at 3840x2160 (with the debug layer on).
  - **The generated frames** (`fgcheck.py` on dumps, `launcher/dev/fg/fgdump-d3d12.pad`): of the pixels equal in both neighbouring real frames, the share the generated frame keeps within 2 codes:
    - standing still: 100% at native; 99.1 to 99.7% at 3x in a 1080p window (2:1) and 99.5 to 99.8% in a 2560x1440 one (1.5:1), compared at the size shown;
    - turning: 97.6 to 99.9%;
    - driving: 94.5 to 96.5% at native and 95.7% at 3x in HDR, 79 to 87% in SDR (one 8-bit step there is about 4 of the 10-bit codes compared);
    - at most 0.15% of the pixels outside what the neighbours hold within 6 pixels, so no garbage or wrong colours.
    - Opaque UI identity (`fgcheck.py --ui-rect x,y,w,h`, `launcher/dev/fg/fg-ui-identity.pad`): the dev pad's test dialog (its background is opaque) is shown while 10 frames are dumped on Vulkan at 1920x1080, with and without HDR, and the rectangle inside its border is the same bit for bit in every one of the 10 generated frames and its real frame (a rectangle of the scene: none). Direct3D 12 can't be checked this way: its dumps are the guest output, which AMD's swap chain puts the UI over afterwards.
    - Two scaling choices were rejected on these checks: scaling the generated frame up to 4K to compose it and back down (only 82 to 84% kept standing still), and averaging the HUD-less image and the UI over boxes while the real frame is scaled bilinearly (different at ratios other than 2:1).
  - **Transitions** (`fg-mode.pad`):
    - the 1 s suspension and its resumption;
    - fullscreen and back: 3 to 7 refreshes not shown per switch. AMD's `ResizeBuffers` drains its presents, stops its threads and makes its buffers again during the UI thread's reconnect (about 60 ms), against about a frame with DXGI's own; the newest frame waits in the mailbox meanwhile;
    - HDR off: presented without generation at 146 a second, presents per swap 1.0;
    - HDR on again.
  - **The Direct3D 12 debug layer** over the same at native, and at 3x through fullscreen switches and an HDR switch with dumps: no errors. AMD's swap chain waits on fence value 0 at times (FENCE_ZERO_WAIT warnings).
  - **Frame generation off:** the default path is unchanged (presents per swap 1.0000), and Vulkan's frame-generation mode still presents every frame once.
- **Checked, phase 2** (Direct3D 12, native, 1920x1080 window, the present log; PresentMon was unavailable for the last runs):
  - **SDR with FSR, HDR with MSAA, SDR with anti-aliasing off:** generated, presents per swap 2.0000 while driving, turning and standing (`fgdump-d3d12.pad`), fgcheck as above.
  - **The pause menu** (the world drawn again behind it, the camera turning around the car): its anti-aliasing draw is there, so it's generated, and 100% of still pixels are kept.
  - **`fg-mode.pad`,** HDR with FSR and SDR with MSAA: every real frame presented once with a generated frame (120 rendered, 240 shown), through the overlay, the suspension, fullscreen switches and HDR off and on; Vulkan's frame-generation mode and Direct3D 12 without generation unchanged.
  - The presentation measurements PresentMon couldn't take then are below.
- **Checked, presentation and latency** (Direct3D 12, HDR, FSR anti-aliasing, 240 Hz G-SYNC display, PresentMon and the present log, `pmgate.pad`'s 40 s of driving, 2026-10-17). Every run was presented in independent flip (a run composed by DWM, "Composed: Flip", doesn't count: PresentMon's display times are only the display's in independent flip). Swap to screen is each real frame's time from its swap to its first scan-out (PresentMon's display latency).

  | | Rendered / shown a second | Swap to screen ms (median, p99) | Displayed ms (median, p1 to p99) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Native, off, uncapped | 145.8 / 145.8 | 2.06, 3.32 | 6.84 (5.5 to 8.4) |
  | Native, off, capped at 116 | 116.0 / 116.0 | 1.08, 1.88 | 8.62 (7.4 to 9.9) |
  | Native, on, uncapped | 120.0 / 240.0 | 22.57, 24.67 | 4.17 (4.12 to 4.21) |
  | Native, on, capped at 116 | 116.0 / 232.0 | 6.30, 8.53 | 4.25 (2.8 to 5.7) |
  | Native, on, `frame_generation_present=tearing` | 120.0 / 240.0 | 17.98, 19.04 | 4.17 (3.3 to 5.1) |
  | 3x window, off, uncapped | 114.2 / 114.2 | 22.53, 24.98 | 8.74 (6.3 to 11.5) |
  | 3x window, on, uncapped | 91.3 / 182.6 | 23.90, 26.80 | 5.71 (3.3 to 8.2) |
  | 3x window, on, capped at 80 | 80.0 / 160.0 | 15.02, 16.26 | 6.17 (4.2 to 8.8) |
  | 3x borderless fullscreen (3840x2160), on, uncapped | 83.4 / 166.8 | 26.49, 29.78 | 6.07 (3.9 to 8.5) |
  | 3x borderless fullscreen, off, uncapped | 117.6 / 117.6 | 21.78, 23.48 | 8.70 (8.1 to 9.7) |

  - **Uncapped, frame generation fills the display:** native reaches the 240 presents a second this display shows, and frames then queue behind the presents: 22.6 ms from swap to screen against 2.1 ms without generation, at about the same rendered rate as a cap of 116 that takes it down to 6.3 ms. With `frame_generation_present=tearing` it still stops at 120 rendered (18 ms). This machine's global NVIDIA profile has a frame rate limiter of 240, which caps the presents too and so meets frame generation the same way; it is the user's setting, read without changing anything.
  - **GPU-bound at 3x,** the game's own queue is already about 22.5 ms without generation (the GPU command thread runs ahead of the GPU); generation adds about 1.4 ms to it, and a cap below the GPU's rate (80) takes it to 15 ms. That queue is low latency's to remove (phase 6), not a cap's: phase 6 measures it with Reflex, generation off and on.
  - **Pacing:** with the cap at native, each present is shown for about one refresh (4.25 ms, against 4.31 ideal). GPU-bound at 3x uncapped, the generated and real frames alternate about 3.5 and 7.7 ms instead of 5.5 each; capped at 80 they spread around the ideal 6.25 ms instead. Borderless fullscreen has variable refresh rate as the window does.
  - **The cap with frame generation** (phase 4): the game setting `mcla_fps_frame_generation` (`src/frame_timing.cpp`), used while frame generation is on and can run; `mcla_fps` (the player's own cap) applies otherwise, and an old saved `mcla_fps` stays as it is. -1 is HALF DISPLAY, the default: floor of 97% of half the display's refresh rate, 116 rendered and 232 shown at 240 Hz, 58 and 116 at 120 Hz, 69 at 144 Hz, 80 at 165 Hz, from the refresh rate Windows reports for the display the window is on (read at start and every two seconds). 0 is uncapped, and a rendered rate from 30 to 240 is a cap of its own (60 shows 120). A higher cap stays possible; the pause menu's labels (`N (2N SHOWN)`) and the launcher's tip say it adds latency (22 ms from swap to screen at an uncapped native 240 Hz against 6 ms at 116).
  - **Under 100 Hz,** or where the refresh rate isn't known (never read on Linux and the PS5 yet), HALF DISPLAY is not offered and the player's own cap applies (`mcla_fps`; the pause menu lists the same caps with what they show): half of 60 or 75 would be under 50 rendered, which looks choppy between the generated frames, and the review's finding F-M7 asked for it to stay out of fixed-refresh displays. Frame generation there is untested; its row and tip say it wants 120 Hz or more, or a variable-refresh display.
- **Checked, phase 3** (Vulkan, Windows, RTX 5080, 1920x1080 window, 240 Hz G-SYNC display, PresentMon and the present log, `fg-mode.pad`, `pmgate.pad` and `race.pad`, 2026-10-24).
  - **Every guest frame presented with a generated frame:** presents per swap 2.0000 (1.995 to 2.002 in a run's segments, except around switches) at native in HDR with FSR, in SDR with MSAA, and at 3x in SDR with MSAA (99.5 rendered and 199.0 shown), through steady driving, the F4 overlay, the forced stall (the UI thread paints meanwhile, as before) and after it, and the race (`race.pad`: the menus, loading, intro and 45 s of racing, 119.9 rendered and 239.9 shown, every real frame once). A fullscreen or HDR switch loses 1 to 6 refreshes, as on Direct3D 12 (1.965 to 1.989 presents per swap in the 6 s around one); every drop is logged.
  - **The generated frames** (`fgcheck.py` on `fg_dump_vulkan_*` dumps, which are the presented images at the window's size: HDR in 10-bit codes, SDR in the swap chain's 8-bit ones; `fgdump-d3d12.pad`'s driving, turning and standing): of the pixels equal in both neighbouring real frames, the share kept within 2 codes:
    - standing: 99.0 to 100%;
    - turning: 97.4 to 99.99% (the lowest is 3x in HDR with MSAA);
    - driving: 95.5 to 96.6% in HDR with FSR at native and 3x, 92.8 to 93.9% with MSAA, 97.0 to 98.7% in SDR (8-bit codes);
    - the pause menu: 99.997 to 100%;
    - 3x in a 2560x1440 window (1.5:1, generated at the window's size) through fullscreen, windowed and HDR switches: 96.1% driving, 99.6 to 99.9% otherwise;
    - at most 0.13% of the pixels are outside what the neighbours hold within 6 pixels (0.03% in HDR), so no garbage or wrong colours.
  - **Anti-aliasing switched while generating:** FSR to MSAA to off and back to FSR while driving (`launcher/dev/fg/fg-aa-switches.pad`): a generated frame with every real one in all four states (the vectors alone without FSR), every real frame presented once, 99.2 to 99.8% of the still pixels kept, nothing in the log.
  - **Pacing and latency against Direct3D 12** (`pmgate.pad`'s 40 s of driving, HDR, FSR anti-aliasing, independent flip in every run; a Direct3D 12 row marked * was measured in the same session, the others are the table above):

    | | | Rendered / shown a second | Swap to screen ms (median, p99) | Displayed ms (median, p1 to p99) |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | Native, uncapped | Vulkan | 119.9 / 239.9 | 19.05, 20.16 | 4.17 (3.06 to 5.26) |
    | | Direct3D 12* | 120.0 / 240.0 | 20.25, 22.14 | 4.17 (4.11 to 4.22) |
    | Native, capped at 116 | Vulkan | 116.0 / 232.0 | 6.45, 8.06 | 4.20 (2.76 to 6.18) |
    | | Direct3D 12 | 116.0 / 232.0 | 6.30, 8.53 | 4.25 (2.8 to 5.7) |
    | Native, tearing | Vulkan | 119.9 / 239.7 | 18.93, 19.79 | 4.16 (2.20 to 6.10) |
    | | Direct3D 12 | 120.0 / 240.0 | 17.98, 19.04 | 4.17 (3.3 to 5.1) |
    | 3x window, uncapped | Vulkan | 86.0 / 172.1 | 32.26, 38.96 | 5.66 (4.28 to 9.07) |
    | | Direct3D 12* | 93.0 / 186.0 | 23.40, 26.10 | 5.26 (3.16 to 8.02) |
    | 3x window, capped at 80 | Vulkan | 80.0 / 160.0 | 12.22, 18.54 | 5.86 (2.79 to 9.74) |
    | | Direct3D 12 | 80.0 / 160.0 | 15.02, 16.26 | 6.17 (4.2 to 8.8) |
    | 3x borderless fullscreen (3840x2160), uncapped | Vulkan | 83.6 / 167.2 | 34.09, 39.57 | 5.79 (3.51 to 8.80) |
    | | Direct3D 12 | 83.4 / 166.8 | 26.49, 29.78 | 6.07 (3.9 to 8.5) |
    | Generation off: native, uncapped | Vulkan | 145.9 | | 6.85 (5.28 to 8.47) |
    | Generation off: 3x window, uncapped | Vulkan | 109.5 | 15.04, 25.04 | 9.11 (8.62 to 9.81) |

    (The Vulkan rows are from before the review's fix round, which changed the pacer: at 3x it now renders 90.9 a second, see below.)

    - **Native:** the same as Direct3D 12 in rate and in swap to screen (1.2 ms better uncapped, 0.15 ms and 0.95 ms worse capped and with tearing), tear-free with FIFO and variable refresh rate: every present is displayed for about one refresh. The display times spread more than AMD's (p1 to p99 3.1 to 5.3 ms against 4.11 to 4.22 uncapped): the pacer sleeps and spins on one thread where AMD's hybrid spin has two at the highest priority. The cap (116) and the display hide that in the median.
    - **`frame_generation_present=tearing`:** the first version rendered only 100 a second (the pacer's interval, measured from its own presents, kept the rate where a slow moment had left it); the interval is now shortened where the pacer was behind (see Pacing), and the rate reaches the display's 240 presents.
    - **3x is GPU-bound** (98% busy, 11.4 ms a swap): generation costs 21% of the rate on Vulkan (109.5 to 86.0) and 19% on Direct3D 12 (114.2 to 91.3 on 2026-10-17, 93.0 now), and the swap to screen grows from 15.0 to 32.3 ms (Direct3D 12's game queue was already about 22.5 ms without generation, and 23.4 with). That is an extra rendered frame in the queue and the real frame's half interval; a cap below the GPU's rate (80) takes it to 12.2 ms. Removing that queue is low latency's (phase 6), on both APIs.
    - **GPU time of generation** (`frame_generation_timing`: preparation, generation and compose): 0.39 ms at native (0.27 to 0.48), 1.25 ms at 3x (generated at 1920x1080 from a 3840x2160 render; 0.89 to 1.72). Direct3D 12's (generation and compose; its preparation is in the refresher): 0.35 and 1.10 ms.
  - **Vulkan validation with synchronization validation** (`VK_LAYER_VALIDATE_SYNC=1`, `VK_LAYER_SYNCVAL_SHADER_ACCESSES_HEURISTIC=1`, `VK_LAYER_ENABLE_MESSAGE_LIMIT=0`; a steady drive, the F4 overlay, fullscreen, HDR off and on, at native and 3x, about 180,000 messages each): only what the default path reports (`ShaderOutputNotConsumed`, write-after-write hazards between compute dispatches into two buffers, `vkCmdEndQuery` 07007 and 01923, FSR's `Undefined-Value-StorageImage-FormatMismatch-ImageView`), and one a context from FidelityFX's own descriptor pool (`AllocateDescriptorSets-WrongType`, a storage buffer binding its pool wasn't sized for). It found two faults here, both fixed: the generation images had no transfer usage (FidelityFX copies the HUD-less image: 1,816 barriers and 908 copies in a run), and the SDR refresher's second dispatch (the shader can write the output the first one wrote) had no barrier before it.
  - **The PS5's static FidelityFX path and the prebuilt Vulkan shader set** compile with the frame generation file (`-fsyntax-only` with `REX_FSR_STATIC` and with `REX_FSR_PREBUILT_SHADERS_VK`); not run.
  - **Not checked:** other GPUs and drivers (NVIDIA only), fixed-refresh displays, the failure path (a generation that fails on the submission thread gives generation up, logged once).
- **Checked, the review's fix round** (Vulkan and Direct3D 12, 1920x1080 window, 2026-10-24). PresentMon saw no present from any process that night (the session was locked), so the numbers are from the present log and the pacer's own counters: `frame_generation_timing` now also logs, every 300 real frames, the pacer's presents and the time between its present calls.
  - **Generation made to fail** (`frame_generation_debug_fail`; native, uncapped, `pmgate.pad`'s 40 s of driving; rendered a second, and the median paint of the guest output thread's, as without frame generation: 80 us on Vulkan, 148 us on Direct3D 12):

    | | Vulkan | Direct3D 12 |
    |---|---|---|
    | Generation off | 142.9, 80 us | 142.8, 148 us |
    | Generation on | 120.0, FIFO, the pacer | |
    | The context can't be created (1) | 142.9, 80 us, no pacer presents | 142.9, 149 us |
    | A generation fails (2) | 142.8, 83 us, no pacer presents | 143.0, 152 us |

    Each logs why once, and the connection is made again without frame generation within a few frames: Vulkan back to its usual present mode (IMMEDIATE, no second queue's pacer), Direct3D 12 to a plain DXGI swap chain. The game's GPU use over the whole run (the GPU guard): Vulkan 47% off, 48% on, 49 and 52% failed; Direct3D 12 51%, 51 and 52%.
  - **Presents** (the counters): 2.0000 presents per real frame in every window of 300 frames at native (32 of 37 in `fg-mode.pad`; the others are around the start and the reconnects, 1.76 to 1.9967) and at 3x. At native in FIFO the present calls are 4.17 ms apart (p1 4.03 to 4.10, p99 4.23 to 4.48), the same with `frame_generation_present=tearing` (119.97 rendered a second, 4.17 ms, p1 4.09, p99 4.25). At 3x (GPU-bound) they are 5.36 ms apart (p1 5.27, p99 5.79) and 90.9 are rendered a second (86.0 before the fix round); the interval between real presents is 11.16 ms (p1 10.54, p99 13.68, mean deviation 0.34 ms; before 11.55, 8.31 to 15.18, 1.25). The pacer thread's priority and timer: at native the real presents are 8.33 ms apart (p1 8.29, p99 8.44, mean deviation 0.013 ms; before 8.68 and 0.019).
  - **`fg-mode.pad`** (Vulkan, HDR, FSR): every real frame presented once through the steady drive, the F4 overlay, the stall and after it; 1 to 2 refreshes lost at each of the fullscreen and HDR switches, no connection made twice for nothing.
  - **The generated frames** (`fgcheck.py`, the same drive, turn and stand): native HDR with FSR 97.2 to 97.5% kept in motion, 99.83 to 99.87% turning, 99.998 to 100% standing, 0.03% outside; 3x in HDR with FSR 94.8 to 94.9% in motion, 97.9 to 99.0% turning, 99.0% standing, 0.03% outside; and Direct3D 12 at native after the same change (generation, the UI separation and the reconnect path are the presenter's there): 19 frames checked, 93.4% at the lowest in motion, 99.76 to 100% otherwise, at most 0.18% outside.

The cost of a switch is above.

  - **HALF DISPLAY** (`mcla_fps_frame_generation` = -1 with `--mcla_fps=45`, `pmgate.pad`): 116.01 rendered a second on both APIs at 240 Hz, presents per real frame 2.0000, present calls 4.21 ms apart on Vulkan; the cap log line shows 116 when it is turned on in the pause menu and 45 (mcla_fps) when it is turned off again.

A file with those two lines left by it still starts, ignores them, and the pause menu's row isn't greyed. The same Save used to write `temporal_aa` and `temporal_aa_available` too, which FSR anti-aliasing's patch sets the same way: both are transient now (`rexglue-cvar-toml-strings.patch`), and the Save writes only `frame_generation`.

  - **Every guest frame presented exactly once:** in steady driving, with the F4 overlay open and after a stall (146 a second with the overlay, against 77 on the default path).
  - **The suspension** (after 1 s with frame generation): during a forced 3 s stall the UI thread painted the overlay about 540 times. The mode resumed 250 ms after guest frames came again, the frame at the switch going through the pacer, with no paint without UI around either switch.
  - **Reconnects:** each lost one guest frame per resize or HDR switch on D3D12, and up to five on Vulkan's fullscreen switches (the default path loses none at an HDR switch).
  - **No stale image after a switch:** the frames the Vulkan pacer presented right after fullscreen switches, read back, are whole frames at the new size.
  - **The pacer:**
    - on its own queue (family 0, queue 1), with as many presents in the present log as PresentMon counted;
    - with `frame_generation_debug_pacer_queue=other_family`, in the compute family (2): presents per swap 1.0000;
    - with `none`: "can't present on this connection", painting as without it, presents per swap 1.0000.

A render pass incompatibility it found (the present images' pass) was fixed. The default path with the F4 overlay open also reports `vkQueueSubmit` present-semaphore reuse (00067), from Xenia's per-submission present semaphores. It shows here only while the UI thread presents during a suspension.

## rexglue-vulkan-paint-pipeline-format.patch

**Why.** The Vulkan presenter keeps the pipeline that draws the guest output into the swapchain with the format of the render pass it was made for, and makes it again when the swapchain's format changes (HDR on or off). That format was never recorded: `GuestOutputPaintPipeline::swapchain_format` stayed `VK_FORMAT_UNDEFINED`, so every paint after the first found the pipeline stale. It then waited on the CPU for the GPU to finish the paint before it (`AwaitSubmissionCompletion`), destroyed the pipeline and made it again.
- That earlier paint is queued behind the game's own GPU work, so the wait grows with GPU load. On the GPU command thread (the default way of painting) it took about 4 ms of every frame at 3×.
- On the UI thread it took about two game frames, and the guest output mailbox keeps only the newest frame, so only every other frame reached the screen. The UI thread paints while any UI drawer is registered: upstream's always-registered achievement toast, the F4 settings overlay or the console without `present_ui_overlay`, or Vulkan with FIFO.
- The same code is in Xenia, which the presenter comes from: `src/xenia/ui/vulkan/vulkan_presenter.cc` L1700 in xenia-project/xenia master (fe1fd36, the file's last change), with the member declared at `src/xenia/ui/vulkan/vulkan_presenter.h` L328 and never assigned.

**What it changes.** The format is recorded when the pipeline is made, so the pipeline is made again only when the swapchain's format changes.

**PS5:** applied by `ps5/make_ps5.sh` as well, with the patches after it. Tested by upstream on a PS5 Pro (firmware 13.42) in its own build, which doesn't have the fork's other patches: the game runs without visible problems, at about the same frame rate as without it. Not run on the console with the fork's build.

**Measured** on Windows, Vulkan, RTX 5080 and Ryzen 7 9800X3D, with a present log, as frames shown. The private measurement builds had the fix behind a switch, so each pair is the same build; one run each.
- Fork `a6ee6d9`, race timedemo at 3×:
  - The paint on the GPU command thread: 4.18 → 0.20 ms.
  - The paint on the UI thread (`host_present_from_non_ui_thread=false`): 19.8 → 0.18 ms. Frames shown went from 50.5 a second (every other one of 101 made) to 100.2 (all of them).
  - The default's frame rate didn't change (99.5 and 98.3 a second): the GPU is the limit there, busy 97% of the time with the wait or without it. At native one of the game's threads is the limit (145 either way).
  - In HDR, the default: the paint 4.42 → 0.08 ms, frames shown 96 → 102 a second.
- Upstream's SDK (`54a5569`), city free roam, frames shown a second at native and at 3×:
  - With PR 1 (presenting from the GPU command thread): 61 → 67 and 36 → 41.
  - Painting from the UI thread: 35 → 68 and 21 → 40.
  - Upstream itself, with its always-registered achievement toast: 35 → 51 and 21 → 40. Its repaints now run at the monitor's rate, and at native they hold the game back as they do on D3D12.

**Checked** on fork main with this patch (Windows, RTX 5080), with `launcher/dev/scripts/display-switches.pad` and the validation layers on. HDR off and on and fullscreen on and off, with no overlay, the F4 overlay and the console, on Vulkan with IMMEDIATE and with FIFO. Both runs ended by itself and swaps rose at every step. Every guest frame was presented in every step, the ones after each HDR switch (a new swapchain format) included, and with the overlays open, where the UI thread now presents 240 times a second. The validation layers reported nothing about pipelines or render passes. The present semaphore reuse they did report (`VUID-vkQueueSubmit-pSignalSemaphores-00067`, from before this patch) is the next patch's. On the race at 3× they reported nothing, in either way of painting.

**Upstream:** merged there as PR 23 (from the fork's `pr/02-paint-pipeline-format`), so it is in upstream's `main`. Upstream's copy of the patch is made for an SDK with only its own patches, so its hunk sits earlier in the file and its comment is worded differently; the change is the same lines. This copy is the one the fork's scripts apply and record (an SDK that has it keeps it; a changed file would have to be applied afresh).

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| The final pipeline's swapchain format recorded | `src/ui/vulkan/vulkan_presenter.cpp` | Vulkan (all platforms) |

## rexglue-vulkan-present-semaphore-per-image.patch

**Why.** A paint's submission signals a semaphore, and its `vkQueuePresentKHR` waits for it. The presenter had one per paint submission (three), so a paint could signal one again while the present three paints before still waited for it. Vulkan doesn't allow that (`VUID-vkQueueSubmit-pSignalSemaphores-00067`): nothing tells when a present's wait is done. It happened whenever the UI thread presents at the display's rate (the F4 overlay or the console open, FIFO), from Xenia's presenter.

**What it changes.** One present semaphore per swapchain image, as the frame pacer's are. An image is acquired again only after its previous present, so its semaphore is free by then. They are kept across swapchains (a new swapchain only adds to them; the old one's presents are awaited before it's destroyed) and destroyed with the presenter.

**Checked:** on fork main with both patches, with `display-switches.pad` and the validation layers on Vulkan with IMMEDIATE and with FIFO. `VUID-vkQueueSubmit-pSignalSemaphores-00067`, reported up to the layers' limit (10) in each run before, no longer appears. Both runs ended by themselves with swaps rising at every step. The layers' only other messages were the start-up `vkCmdEndQuery` ones from the GPU plugin, which were there before (Windows, RTX 5080).

**Upstream:** with `rexglue-vulkan-paint-pipeline-format.patch`, in the Vulkan performance PR (PR 4).

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| A present semaphore per swapchain image | `include/rex/ui/vulkan/presenter.h`, `src/ui/vulkan/vulkan_presenter.cpp` | Vulkan (all platforms) |

## rexglue-d3d12-storage-preload-threads.patch

**Why.** At start the D3D12 pipeline cache creates every pipeline in the storage file before the game runs (`InitializeShaderStorage`, blocking). It should start extra creation threads, up to one per logical processor besides the calling one, and then wait for all of them.
- The loop that starts them compared the thread count with itself (`creation_thread_original_count` in place of `creation_thread_needed_count`), so it never ran.
- The wait for the pipelines still being created sat in the branch that shuts the extra threads down, so it never ran either. The preload returned while the creation threads were still working on the last pipelines.
- Xenia master has the same loop (`src/xenia/gpu/d3d12/pipeline_cache.cc` L580); xenia-canary compares with the needed count.

**What it changes.** The extra threads are started (15 in place of 12 on a 16-thread CPU, plus the calling thread). A blocking preload waits for every pipeline being created, also when no extra thread was needed (CPUs with few logical processors).

**Measured** on Windows, RTX 5080 and Ryzen 7 9800X3D. The pipelines were the user's 1,684 (RTV) from a copy of their storage, and the old behaviour sat behind a switch in a private measurement build. Alternating runs; for cold runs a shader salt made the driver's cache miss.
- **Cold, 7 runs a side:** every pipeline created after 982 ms (median; 850–1,083) against 1,039 ms (948–1,133). Faster in all 7 pairs, by 1.4–15% (1.07× on average).
- **The old preload returned too early:** in 6 of the 7 cold runs it returned with 2–9 pipelines still being created. The fixed one never did.
- **Warm, 3 runs a side:** 48 ms against 50–52 ms, the old one returning with 3–7 still being created.

**Upstream:** with the stutter changes (PR 5).

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| The storage preload's extra creation threads and its final wait | `src/graphics/d3d12/pipeline_cache.cpp` | D3D12 |

## rexglue-vulkan-pipeline-storage-keep-unsupported.patch

**Why.** At start the Vulkan pipeline cache reads the pipeline descriptions in the storage file and skips those the device can't create (`ArePipelineRequirementsMet`: features another GPU or driver has), so that the file stays shareable. It then truncated the file to the number it kept. So for every unsupported description anywhere in the file, a valid one was cut off its end, at every start, and the whole list if none was supported.

**What it changes.** The file is truncated only after the last valid description (a corrupted one still cuts the rest). Unsupported descriptions stay in it for devices that can create them, and are only skipped at load.

**Checked** with a private switch that made every stored description whose hash is divisible by 4 unsupported at load: 114 of the user's 472 Vulkan pipelines, from a copy of their storage.
- **Before:** the first start cut the file to 358 descriptions. 86 of the 114 lost were supported ones, and the next normal start created 358.
- **With this patch:** the file kept all 472. The first start created 358 and the next one all 472.

**Upstream:** with the stutter changes (PR 5).

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| Unsupported pipeline descriptions kept in the storage | `src/graphics/vulkan/pipeline_cache.cpp` | Vulkan (all platforms) |

## rexglue-shader-storage-prepare.patch

**Why.** The fork's shader pack import and preparation (`src/shader_prepare.cpp`) add shaders and pipeline descriptions to the player's shader storage, then let the SDK create every stored pipeline, and need from the SDK:
- the versions the storage files must carry: a file whose header holds another one is emptied at start, so the game checks its shader pack against them before it writes anything;
- how far the start-up creation of the stored pipelines got, for the launcher's progress line;
- what the storage was loaded with, for the record the launcher compares before Play (the stamp): the API, the pipeline storage file in use (which says the EDRAM path the GPU code chose: render targets or rasterizer-ordered views on D3D12, framebuffer or fragment shader interlock on Vulkan), the adapter and its driver, the resolution scale;
- in a preparation run, which ends without the graphics shutdown, the storage closed first, so that a driver pipeline cache (`vulkan_pipeline_cache_file`) is saved.

**What it changes.**
- `include/rex/graphics/shader_storage.h`: the shader storage's and both pipeline storages' versions, as public constants. The D3D12 and Vulkan pipeline caches `static_assert` that they equal their own.
- `shader_storage_progress` (0, off, by default): while the stored pipelines are created at start, `SHADERPREP preload <done> <total>` on standard output every that many pipelines, and `SHADERPREP preload-done <created> <total> <ms>` at the end. D3D12 counts every stored description (created, failed, or skipped because its shaders aren't there) during a blocking preload, and prints nothing for one that doesn't block; Vulkan counts the pipelines it creates. Nothing else changes with it off.
- `IGraphicsSystem::ShaderStorageReport(bool shutdown)` (a default that returns nothing, so other graphics systems need nothing): a JSON object with the API, the pipeline storage file, how many descriptions it held and how many pipelines were created from it, the adapter (D3D12: its description, vendor, device, subsystem and revision, and the user-mode driver's version from `CheckInterfaceSupport`; Vulkan: the device's name, vendor and device, `driverVersion` and `pipelineCacheUUID`), the resolution scale and the EDRAM size. It runs on the command processor's thread, as `InitializeShaderStorage`. With `shutdown`, the pipeline cache's `ShutdownShaderStorage` runs first (the storage writer joined, the files closed, a dirty `VkPipelineCache` saved).

**Retiring stale entries needs nothing more from the SDK** (2026-10-25; the game does it, `src/shader_prepare.cpp`, `RetireStale`). It relies on how the storage already behaves:
- The files are open (`a+b`) only from `InitializeShaderStorage` to the graphics shutdown, and the game's retirement runs before the first and replaces files whole, atomically, under its session lock, so the SDK never has a file open that is replaced.
- Pipelines whose shaders aren't in the shader file, or whose shader translation is invalid, are skipped at the preload, not an error: so removing a shader from the file only skips the descriptions that still use it (the game removes a shader only when no description in any of the four lists uses it), and a removed description is simply created, and appended again, the next time the game draws it.
- Appended entries are never deduplicated in the file (D3D12's preload skips a description it already has in memory), which is why the retirement compares keys and not positions.
- Both caches truncate a pipeline list to its last valid record at the start; the game's rewrites keep every record whole, so that never cuts a retirement.
The creation of the stored pipelines (a counter, `shader_storage_progress`, and the stamp's `created` against `descriptions`) is also what shows a pack's entries are all usable on a build: on this build every list of the pack of 2026-10-17 was created whole (2,928 of 2,928 D3D12 rtv, 570 of 570 rov, 1,561 of 1,561 Vulkan fbo, 474 of 474 fsi), so there was no description to retire as unusable. The SDK doesn't say which descriptions it skipped; a pack with a count lower than its descriptions' would need that (not done).

**Upstream:** fork-only for now (it serves the fork's shader pack).

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| The storage versions, public | `include/rex/graphics/shader_storage.h`, `src/graphics/d3d12/pipeline_cache.cpp`, `src/graphics/vulkan/pipeline_cache.cpp` | all platforms |
| Progress of the stored pipelines' creation | `src/graphics/shader_storage_progress.h`, `src/graphics/command_processor.cpp`, `include/rex/graphics/d3d12/pipeline_cache.h`, `src/graphics/d3d12/pipeline_cache.cpp`, `src/graphics/vulkan/pipeline_cache.cpp` | D3D12, Vulkan (all platforms) |
| The storage report, and closing the storage on request | `include/rex/system/interfaces/graphics.h`, `include/rex/graphics/graphics_system.h`, `src/graphics/graphics_system.cpp`, `include/rex/graphics/command_processor.h`, the D3D12 and Vulkan `command_processor.h/.cpp` and `pipeline_cache.h/.cpp` | D3D12, Vulkan (all platforms) |

## rexglue-content-header-padding.patch

**Why.** `ContentManager::WriteContentHeaderFile` (`src/system/xam/content_manager.cpp`) wrote a whole `XCONTENT_AGGREGATE_DATA` with one `fwrite`. The struct (0x148 bytes) has two gaps its alignment pads, 4 bytes before `xuid` (offsets 308 to 311) and 4 after `title_id` (324 to 327), which nothing sets: they held whatever was on the stack. Builds with MSVC happened to leave zeros; Clang for mingw leaves stack bytes. The same save then wrote a different header file (`Headers/<type>/<name>.header`) from one build to the next, which showed up in an A/B comparison of two builds' output. The game reads the header back as a whole struct and the padding is never used, so nothing misbehaved.

**What it changes.** The struct is copied into a byte array and the two gaps are zeroed (found from the offsets of `xuid` and `title_id`, not hard-coded) before the write. Old header files load as before (the reader copies the bytes it finds); one written by an older build keeps the bytes it has until the game writes it again.

**Checked** (Windows, `launcher/dev/scripts/content-header.pad`: the dev pad's `write_content_header` calls the function with a struct filled with 0xEE first, so any padding that was copied would show): the header file has zeros at 308 to 311 and 324 to 327, and the title id at 320 to 323. An older header with 0xAB and 0xCD in those bytes, made by hand in a copy of the save data, still loads: the game reached the city with the career save, and the file was left as it was.

**Upstream:** a candidate for upstream ReXGlue.

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| The content header file written with zero padding | `src/system/xam/content_manager.cpp` | all platforms |

## rexglue-user-name.patch

**Why.** The SDK signs the player in as a profile called "User" whatever they are called (`UserProfile` set `name_ = "User"`), and the game shows that name where it shows the player's own (it asks `XamUserGetName`, which copies at most 15 characters and the terminator, as an Xbox gamertag has 15). A player asked to be able to change it (fork issue #14).

**What it changes.** A `user_name` setting (default `User`; the command line, `REX_USER_NAME` or `mcla.toml`; restart to apply) that `UserProfile` takes its name from: the spaces around it dropped, then 1 to 15 ASCII letters, digits and spaces, as a gamertag is made of. Anything else is ignored with a warning in the log (`user_name '...' is not a gamertag ...: using 'User'`) and the name is `User`, so a mistyped name never breaks the profile. The start-up log says which name and XUID the profile has (`user profile: name 'Zed', XUID B13EBABEBABEBABE`). Nothing else about the profile moves with the name: the XUID (`0xB13EBABEBABEBABE`, which the game checks and which names the folder of the saves and achievements) is the same for every name, and `ContentManager::ResolveGameUserContentPath` (where a game's title-specific profile settings go, `<root>/<title id>/profile/<name>`) used the profile's name as its folder, so it is fixed to `User`, the folder it always had: those settings stay found when the name changes. mcla's launcher has a *Player name* box that writes the setting to `mcla.toml` (`launcher/src/GameSettings.cs`, `PlayerName`).

**Checked.** The patch applies to the pristine v0.10.0 files and, in `make_pc.ps1`'s order, to the patched SDK (`scripts/ci/check_pins.py`). The runs on Windows (a name in the log, where the game shows it, a save loading with the profile under another name, names the SDK refuses) are written in `launcher/dev/player-name-2026-10-27.md`.

**Upstream:** a candidate for upstream ReXGlue (a setting for the profile's name).

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| `user_name`: the profile's name from a setting, validated as a gamertag | `src/system/xam/user_profile.cpp` | all platforms |
| The profile-settings folder keeps the name `User` | `src/system/xam/content_manager.cpp` | all platforms |

## rexglue-low-latency.patch

**Why.** When the GPU is the limit (3x resolution, and frame generation on top), the game runs ahead of it: the CPU finishes the next frame while the GPU is still on this one, and the frames queue in front of the GPU. Each queued frame is input lag (22 ms from the game's swap to the screen at 3x, uncapped, on an RTX 5080). The GPU vendors' latency reducers take the queue away: the driver holds the game back at the start of a frame, just long enough that the frame meets a free GPU. This patch makes the game ask for them: NVIDIA Reflex (Direct3D 12 through NVAPI, Vulkan through `VK_NV_low_latency2`) and AMD Anti-Lag 2 (Direct3D 12's driver extension, Vulkan's `VK_AMD_anti_lag`). Intel has no equivalent the game can use. **NVIDIA Reflex is on by default** (it costs 4 to 5% of the frame rate at 3x on Direct3D 12 and nothing elsewhere, for 13 to 15 ms less from the game's swap to the screen: below); AMD Anti-Lag 2 is off by default (untested); either is forced on whenever frame generation is on.

**What it does.**
- **Frames and ids.** A guest frame is one pass through the engine timer (the update thread: `LowLatency::OnFrameStart`, called by the game from a hook on the timer's first instruction, `mcla_frame_start`, before the timer reads the time, so that the driver's wait is part of the simulation step it measures and not a gap in it) to one swap packet (written by `VdSwap` on the render thread: `LowLatency::OnGuestSwap`, called from `VdSwap_entry` once the packet is written; executed by the GPU command thread). A frame's id is the ordinal of the swap packet that ends it. The update thread knows it before the swap is written: the packets written so far plus the frames it started that have no packet yet plus one (both counts in one atomic word); the render thread counts the packets where it writes them, the GPU command thread where it executes them, in the same order, so every marker of a frame, from any thread, carries the same id without a queue between the threads, and a swap that the game's `mcla_present_interval` hook does not see (any present interval but 2) cannot shift the ids. A swap with no frame started for it (the logos, loading screens) is not marked. When the update thread runs twice before the render thread swaps (T T S S), the two frames have their own ids; this happens in 0.04 to 0.7% of the swaps in scripted runs (up to 4% in an older uncapped native run with the reducer off; the 350-pair probe that first found the two threads alternating strictly was too short to see it). A frame that was lost (an update the render thread merged into the next one, or a stall) is written off, so the ids don't stay shifted by one (the pass that writes it off can get an id that was already started; its markers are not sent twice). One with no swap for a quarter of a second was lost. One still pending at the next pass after a swap may be, but it is also what an update thread a frame ahead of the swaps looks like, which is the usual state while the game is CPU-bound (at native resolution, uncapped, a frame was waiting at 85% of the timer passes, and the frames and swaps were one to one, 8,574 of each in 60 s): writing that off at once, which this patch did at first, wrote off 981 frames in 60 s there (11%, each one without its markers and the ids that followed one off; replaying the recorded passes and swaps, 72% of the ids were not their swap's ordinal).
