*Bit-exactness against the EDRAM buffer path* (`launcher/dev/direct-resolve-validate.sdk.patch`, `direct_resolve_validate=true`: every Nth direct resolve is also done through the EDRAM buffer and the two results compared). The final shaders (the plugin built with the validation patch, on the Radeon), with the batched loads on and off: **0 differing bytes in 8,150 validated resolves**. Direct3D 12 at 1x, a scripted drive, 1,050 resolves with the loads on and 1,050 with them off; Direct3D 12 at 1x in a race, 3,150 (on); Direct3D 12 at 2x, 800 (on); Vulkan at 1x, 1,050 with the loads on and 1,050 off. Every run covered 15 to 22 kinds of resolve (fast and full copies at 8, 16, 32 and 64 bits; colour with 1, 2 and 4 samples, including `k_2_10_10_10_FLOAT` read as itself and as `k_16_16_16_16_FLOAT`, the averaging copies that skip the second sample's conversion where its raw bits equal the first's; depth with 1, 2 and 4 samples), and every kind had validated resolves and none differing. The full 8 and 16 bit copies run the earlier shaders in both cases. Earlier versions of the direct resolve were compared on the RTX 5080 (`rexglue-direct-resolve.patch`); this one was not run on the RTX.

*Debug layers* (the final shaders, Direct3D 12 debug layer on the Radeon and the RTX 5080 at 1x and the RTX at 3x, 22,561 lines of output): 0 errors, 0 corruption messages; the warnings are the ones the earlier patches had (initial states of buffers ignored, blend factors with MIN and MAX, live objects at exit), nothing about a resolve pipeline, a descriptor or a barrier. Vulkan validation layers, the Radeon and the RTX 5080 at 1x: `VUID-vkCmdEndQuery-None-07007` and `-01923` twice each, `Undefined-Value-ShaderOutputNotConsumed-DynamicRendering` (the earlier runs' ones), and on the RTX `UNASSIGNED-Threading-MultipleThreads-Read` ten times, for `vkSetLatencyMarkerNV` and `vkLatencySleepNV` against `vkQueuePresentKHR` on the same swapchain (Reflex, the extension's own specification allows it, see `rexglue-low-latency.patch`). Synchronization validation was not run on this patch.

**With the FSR upscaling patch.** This patch goes after `rexglue-fsr-upscaling.patch` in the order (it was made on the SDK without it and re-made on top of it when the two were put together). Both add to `resolve_direct.xesli` and to the render target caches' tables, and they do not meet: the stretching resolves of the FSR patch (`kResolveDirectStretchShaders`, `XE_RESOLVE_DIRECT_STRETCH`) keep their own shaders and do not use the batched loads (the batched shaders are made without the stretch constants, and no shader is made with both), and the resolves that are not stretched use the batched ones as before. All 48 resolve shaders of both patches and the earlier ones (stretch, batched, plain) were rebuilt from the merged sources with `launcher/dev/build_resolve_shaders.py --check` and are bit for bit the headers in the patches. Not measured: the two together (a resolve that is stretched does not use the batched loads, so the gain above is for the resolves that are not).

Built and run on Windows only, with an NVIDIA RTX 5080 and the Radeon above; not run on Intel or the PS5 (the Vulkan shaders are the same sources).

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| The setting | `include/rex/graphics/flags.h`, `src/graphics/pipeline/render_target/cache.cpp` | D3D12, Vulkan |
| The batched loads | `src/graphics/shaders/resolve_direct.xesli`, `src/graphics/shaders/resolve_direct_fast_32bpp.xesli` | D3D12, Vulkan |
| 16 shaders (XeSL sources, DXBC and SPIR-V) | `src/graphics/shaders/resolve_direct_batched_*`, `src/graphics/shaders/bytecode/d3d12_5_1/resolve_direct_batched_*_cs.h`, `src/graphics/shaders/vulkan_spirv/resolve_direct_batched_*_cs.h` | D3D12, Vulkan |
| The tables and the choice | `include/rex/graphics/d3d12/render_target_cache.h`, `src/graphics/d3d12/render_target_cache.cpp`, `include/rex/graphics/vulkan/render_target_cache.h`, `src/graphics/vulkan/render_target_cache.cpp` | D3D12, Vulkan |

## rexglue-xma-decoder-fixes.patch

**Why.** Two player reports of the same kind: the radio's balance "goes wild" after a couple of seconds ([#2](https://github.com/RenanQueiroz/mcla-recomp/issues/2): "the same bug with the radio as Xenia; the fix on Xenia Edge was xma_decoder old to new"), and audio that "goes crazy" in cutscenes with reverb and static on voices and radio songs that desync (#7, a 6-core i5-10400 at 80-90 FPS). **Found (2026-10-27):** the SDK's XMA decoder (v0.10.0) loses whole frames in a long stream. The game plays a stereo song as two mono XMA contexts, one per channel; compared with FFmpeg's decode of the same input, the shipped decoder drops a 512-sample frame every 10 to 20 seconds in each channel on its own (6 to 11 frames in the first 95 seconds of the radio, in every run), so the left and right channel drift apart by whole frames (the stereo image wanders, the channels comb against each other, each lost frame is a click) and the song runs ahead of its time. With `xma_canary_fixes` the decode is FFmpeg's, sample for sample, in every run (details under "Checked"); that switch is therefore on by default. The other switches are measured and off unless said otherwise.

**What the SDK has, and what Xenia has.** The SDK's XMA decoder (`src/audio/xma_context.cpp`, since ReXGlue 0.7.1) is not Xenia's old decoder. It is the state-machine one that Xenia Canary calls `new`: Canary replaced its own `XmaContextNew` with one derived from this SDK on 2026-05-11 (Canary `b575c6841`, "derived from the AC6_recomp RexGlue SDK"). So switching "old to new" has no counterpart here; what Canary (default `new` since 2025-12-01) and Xenia Edge (github.com/has207/xenia-edge, the same maintainer) changed after that date is the delta, and it is small: the commits listed below. Their FFmpeg (8.0 base, a rewritten `xmaframes` decoder: no skipped first frame, error handling, a flush callback) decodes a good frame the same way as the SDK's (FFmpeg 4.4 base with Joel Linn's `xmaframes`), so no newer FFmpeg is needed. The Xenia reports of this game (xenia-canary/game-compatibility #145: a channel muted in the radio, music that "doubles", desync fixed by `apu_max_queued_frames` 16 to 64) are of Xenia's old decoder and its queue length; the SDK's decoder has its own faults, found in review and, with the counters of this patch, to be counted.

**What it changes** (`xma_canary_fixes` is on by default; every other cvar defaults to the SDK's behaviour):
- `xma_race_fixes`. (1) `Work()` looks at the context again once it holds the lock: the guest's lock/disable (`Block()` waits only until the lock is free) or release can run while the decoder thread waits for the lock, and the context is then decoded half-written (ForzaHorizon1-ReImagined, patch 0105, MIT). `Release()` also clears a pending kick. (2) `StoreContextMerged()` no longer writes back `output_buffer_read_offset`: the game advances it as it consumes PCM, nothing in `Work()`, `Decode()` or `Consume()` changes it (the one writer, `ClearLocked()` inside `Work()`, left Canary's decoder in `bc5472055`, the write-back stayed), and the value written back was read when `Work()` began, so a decode that overlapped the game's consume rewound the game's read pointer: the same blocks consumed again, the free space computed wrong from then on. Xenia Canary and Edge still have that write-back. (3) The merge is made a 32-bit word at a time with compare-and-swap, retried from the new value when the game changed the word in the meantime. (4) A kick decodes on the kicking thread only and returns when the decode is done; the worker sweep is not woken (before, the worker could win the race for a kicked context, the inline `Work()` returned at once and the game went on while the worker was still decoding, and the worker could decode a second time without a kick). Xenia Edge made the same choice on 2026-08-20 (`1fae8f43a`: "the title's data always current").
- `xma_canary_fixes`. The XMA changes of Xenia Canary and Edge since 2026-05-11 that apply to this decoder (BSD 3-Clause, "Copyright 2024 Xenia Canary"; all by Herman S., has207): `6bef1fbed` `Work()` does not end its loop while a frame is only partly handed out (`Consume()` hands out `subframe_decode_count` blocks per pass, `Work()` clears the enable and only a kick sets it again, so the rest of the frame was lost); `7015473a7` the search for the next frame follows a frameless packet's skip count instead of the next packet (multi-stream files); `aee0871dd` the frame whose 15-bit header crosses a packet boundary is counted (else the next frame is skipped); `5dd1cdbbf` (Edge) a loop start one bit short of a frame boundary is resolved to the frame; `ade7e610b` a decode pass that moves nothing ends the loop (else `Work()` spins on the kicking thread). Not taken: `09dbe2cd3`/`505697f98`/`7e98ae6de`, the output-valid rules written for NFS Carbon and Most Wanted, which discard decoded PCM that has not been consumed when the input is starved. Without these the decoder loses a frame whenever a pass that exhausts an input buffer leaves a frame partly handed out (`6bef1fbed`), or a frame starts in the last bits of a packet (`aee0871dd`): that is the lost frames of "Why". Off restores the SDK's behaviour.
- `audio_thread_priority` (0 to 3; restart). The kernel's thread priorities are ignored (`ignore_thread_priorities` is true), so the "Audio Worker" (it runs the guest's XAudio2 mixer once per 5.33 ms frame) and the "XMA Decoder" thread ran at the same priority as the game's busiest threads. Xenia Edge gives its audio worker a high priority for pacing (`SetPriority(24)`). 1 is above normal, 2 highest, 3 highest and registered with the Windows multimedia scheduler (MMCSS, task "Pro Audio", `avrt.dll` loaded at run time; `src/audio/audio_thread.cpp`), which keeps a light thread ahead of ordinary ones even when every core is busy. Measured on a 16-thread PC with nothing starving the worker: no difference (see Checked).
- `audio_client_lock` (restart). The audio client table (`AudioSystem::clients_`) has its own lock instead of the global critical region, which the guest's interlocked helpers and the heap take, so that a holder of the global lock cannot delay the pump. Xenia Edge, `12e3b4223`.
- `audio_adaptive_queue` (restart), `audio_adaptive_queue_step` (4), `audio_adaptive_queue_max` (24). The SDL driver plays a frame of silence when the queue is empty, and that silence stays in the stream (everything the mixer makes afterwards plays that much later than the picture). With this on, the first underrun after the game's first second of audio lets the mixer run `step` frames further ahead (more semaphore credits), up to `max` frames of 5.33 ms, so a longer stall of the mixer thread is absorbed by the queue instead of becoming a gap and a delay. The price is the extra latency, only after a stall. Canary users reach the same end by setting `apu_max_queued_frames` to 16 to 64 for good.
- Instrumentation, all off: `audio_stats` (counters every `audio_stats_interval` seconds and at exit: XMA kicks, `Work()` calls on the kicking thread and on the worker, their time, what the game wrote into a context during a decode (read offset, valid flags, others), the pump's callback time and the longest gap between two, and the SDL output's underruns (a frame of silence put in because none was queued: they stay in the stream, so the sound runs later against the picture from then on) and the shortest queue), and `audio_dump_dir` (the final mix as 32-bit float `mix.wav` before the mute, each XMA context's decoded PCM as `xma_ctx<N>_s<M>.wav` with the packets it was given as `.xma` and a `.json`; `audio_dump_max_mb` caps the total). The game leaves through `std::_Exit` (window close), which kills every thread and runs no shutdown: the statistics thread is never a joinable global (that would end in `std::terminate`), each `interval` line is followed by a cumulative `TOTAL` line (the last one is the result), every dump file is a valid WAV after each 256 KB block (the header sizes are patched then, and the `.json` follows), and a static destructor armed by `audio_stats`/`audio_dump_dir` flushes what is left without waiting on any lock and writes the final totals to `audio_stats_total.txt` in the dump folder. The counters that tell the start-up silence from a real underrun: `underruns_steady` and `silence_max` (underruns after the guest's first second of audio, and the longest run of silent frames), `queue_grown`, and a histogram of how long the audio worker takes to start the mixer callback after the device freed a slot (`wake: lt1ms ... ge16ms`, `max_us`). The old `late(>8ms)` pump counter is not a lateness measure: the output device asks for 480 samples every 10 ms (two guest frames at once), so about half of the gaps between two mixer callbacks are 10 ms by construction.
- `rex::audio::dump::SteadyUnderruns()` (always on, one relaxed load of a counter): the silent frames the output has played after the game's first second of audio. They were counted only with `audio_stats`; the add is now unconditional (it runs only in the underrun branch of the SDL callback) so that the game can poll it: `mcla_pump_wake_on_demand` (`src/poll_wait.cpp`) goes back to the game's own pacing when it rises while the frame-pacing thread is coming round early. Nothing else of the patch changes.

**Not the cause, as far as known.** The 5.1 to stereo fold (a fixed linear mix: it cannot make a balance drift), FFmpeg (above), the output queue on the machine measured (a queue of `audio_maxqframes` = 8 frames is 42.7 ms and was never empty after the first second in 16 runs; the shipped build's ~50 underruns per run are all in the first 0.3 s, before the game's mixer has produced anything). A slower CPU may differ: that is what `audio_thread_priority` 3 and `audio_adaptive_queue` are for. They were measured at 4 logical processors (round 2 under "Checked"), where the shipped defaults had no steady underruns either, so they stay off and a weaker CPU than the test PC's is still untested.

**From elsewhere.** Xenia Canary and Xenia Edge, BSD 3-Clause (`Copyright (c) 2015, Ben Vanik.`, the licences of the two repositories are the same file), which the SDK's `LICENSE` already carries in substance: the code of `xma_canary_fixes` (the `consider_frame` scan, the split-header count, the skip chain, the drain condition, the stall check) taken with the authors' comments, adapted to the SDK's names (commits and author above), and the design, not the code, of the kick in `xma_race_fixes` (Edge `1fae8f43a`). ForzaHorizon1-ReImagined patch 0105 by Dyotson (MIT, the notice is in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`): the re-check under the lock and the release clearing the kick; its input-buffer validation is not taken. The rest, the read-offset finding, the compare-and-swap merge, the counters and the dumps, is this fork's work.

**Checked** (a Windows PC with 16 logical processors, D3D12, the route of `launcher/dev/scripts/audio-route.pad`: title, map, city, 25 s of radio at the spawn, a 70 s drive; audio muted for the speakers and dumped before the mute; 16 runs: 12 and 8 logical processors, 30/60/uncapped FPS). The radio pair against FFmpeg's decode of the same input (`launcher/dev/audio/radio_pairs.py`, `radio_all.py`): **shipped decoder** (11 runs: every switch off, race only, priority only): 6 to 11 frames lost in 95 s, 50 to 100 % of the 1024-sample blocks more than 64 LSB off the reference, the first mismatch within the first 26 s of the stream, the left/right correlation at lag 0 about 0 (the best alignment of the two channels is off by 512 to 2048 samples for most of the stream) against 0.76 to 0.95; **with `xma_canary_fixes`** (5 runs: all on, canary only, all on with a 16-frame queue, all on at 8 logical processors): 0 frames lost, 0 to 0.2 % of the blocks off (one block of 4000), correlation 0.76 to 0.95, balance steady (std 0.3 to 0.7 dB). The radio is decoded at wall-clock speed in all of them (1.0005 to 1.0009). The race fixes did not change what the counters show on this PC (the game never wrote into a context during a decode in any run; in the shipped build the XMA worker thread decided 30 to 56 kicked contexts per run, 0 with the race fixes). Output underruns: all at the start of every run (about 50 silent frames, the device runs before the game's mixer), none after the first second except in one 30 FPS run where the whole process stood still for 240 ms once; the queue never dropped below 4 of 8. **Round 2 (2026-10-27, the same route, 16 and 4 logical processors; 4 is two cores with their second threads, a job object's affinity of 0xF; real time, uncapped, D3D12; `launcher/dev/audio-2026-10-27.md` has the table).** With the shipped defaults (`xma_canary_fixes` on, everything else off): **0 steady underruns** (silent frames played after the game's first second of audio) at 16 logical processors and at 4, the longest silence 0 frames, the queue never below 4 of 8 frames at 4, and the radio identical to FFmpeg's decode (0 frames lost, 0.0 % of the blocks off, left/right correlation 0.96 at 16 and 0.93 at 4). With the SDK's decoder (`xma_canary_fixes=false`) at 4 logical processors: **7 radio frames lost** in the 95 s window, 85.5 % of the blocks off, left/right correlation 0.20 (and 3 steady underruns in that run). The other switches changed nothing that could be measured: `audio_thread_priority` 3, `audio_maxqframes` 16, `audio_adaptive_queue` and the best of them together all gave 0 steady underruns at 4 logical processors, as the defaults did; the audio worker's late wakes (8 ms or more after the device freed a queue slot) were 112 of 27,857 at 4 logical and 56 of 25,747 at 16 with the defaults, never more than 10 ms (one device period) and about the same with the switches (127, 80 and 111 at 4), so there was nothing for them to remove on this PC, and they stay off; a CPU weaker than this one's is the case they are for, and it is untested. **The pump-sleep cap** `mcla_pump_sleep_max_ms` (`src/poll_wait.cpp`, the game's thread that sleeps out 7 ms per round; the mixer is paced by the output device, not by that thread) **is not safe on 4 logical processors**: with a cap of 2 ms 859 steady underruns (the longest silence 158 frames, the worst wake 0.85 s, 15 gaps in the mix), with 1 ms 504 (136 frames, 0.73 s, 9 gaps), against 0 with the game's own Sleep, though it raises the frame rate there (160 and 191 FPS in the drive against 140); at 16 logical processors the same caps gave 0 steady underruns (202 and 239 FPS against 145; the pump thread's longest gap was 265 ms once, with 2 ms). It stays -1 (the game's own Sleep) and is not a setting for players. Not checked: how it sounds (the listening clips are in the work folder), a CPU weaker than the test PC's (4 logical processors of this PC are not a slower CPU), a cutscene (the route does not reach one), the audio of the PS5 and Linux builds. The patch applies to ReXGlue v0.10.0 as the build scripts apply it and `check_pins.py` is clean; the sources, with the MMCSS registration, the adaptive queue and the wake histogram, compiled and ran in the round-2 build (11 runs, exit code 0 in all). Nothing of this ran on the PS5 (its build applies the patch like the PC's; `audio_thread.cpp` is empty there, MMCSS being Windows only, and the decoder fix, the adaptive queue and the counters are platform neutral code that is untested on the console).

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| `xma_race_fixes`, `xma_canary_fixes`, the counters of what the game wrote during a decode | `src/audio/xma_context.cpp`, `include/rex/audio/xma/context.h` | XMA decoding, all platforms |
| The kick path, the MMCSS call of the XMA thread | `src/audio/xma_decoder.cpp` | XMA decoding |
| `audio_thread_priority`, `audio_client_lock`, the pump's counters and wake histogram | `src/audio/audio_system.cpp`, `include/rex/audio/audio_system.h` | all platforms |
| MMCSS registration (`audio_thread_priority` 3) | `src/audio/audio_thread.cpp` (new) | Windows |
| `audio_adaptive_queue`, the SDL output's counters and the mix dump | `src/audio/sdl/sdl_audio_driver.cpp`, `include/rex/audio/sdl/sdl_audio_driver.h` | SDL audio (Windows, Linux, the PS5) |
| `audio_stats`, `audio_dump_dir`, the WAV writers | `src/audio/audio_dump.cpp`, `include/rex/audio/audio_dump.h`, `include/rex/audio/flags.h`, `src/audio/CMakeLists.txt` | all platforms |

## rexglue-alpha-to-mask-tolerance.patch

Fork issue #8 ("white texture/block issues"): opaque geometry with a lattice of holes in it, one pixel in every 2x2 quad, through which what is behind shows. The posts of the bus shelter in the story's first cutscene look like a dotted grid against the building behind; a lamp pole in front of a lit shop window, a palm trunk and the bushes in front of the window carry the same dots exactly inside the window's rectangle (outside it the pole's background is as dark as the pole, so the holes cannot be seen). The game draws most of its world with alpha to mask: `RB_COLORCONTROL` 0x8700001C is alpha test (greater than 0) with alpha to mask and the dither offsets 3, 1, 0, 2, and 703 of the 3,730 draws of a city frame at dusk (704 of 3,653 by day) are like that, all in the 2x MSAA scene: the billboards of the distant trees, the shadow collector, foliage and other world draws. The translators make the coverage as the console's hardware does (`registers.h`): sample 0 of a 2x pixel is covered when alpha is at least 0.5 - offset / 8, sample 1 when it is at least 1 - offset / 8, the offset being the one of the pixel's place in its 2x2 quad. The pixel with offset 0 therefore needs alpha of 1.0 exactly for its second sample, and the alpha of many world shaders is a product (a texture's alpha, an interpolated vertex alpha, constants) that comes out a hair under 1.0 on the host's GPU. That pixel then loses its second sample: with 2x MSAA a half-contrast dot where the resolve averages it with what is behind. Xenia Canary has the same code (and a `use_fuzzy_alpha_epsilon` of 1e-3 for the alpha test, "to prevent flickering on NVIDIA graphics cards": the same kind of difference).

Where the dots are: x odd, y even on D3D12 and x even, y odd on Vulkan, which are the same place by each translator's own numbering of the quad (D3D12 2 * (x & 1) + (y & 1), Vulkan (x & 1) + 2 * (y & 1); the two differ, which this patch does not change), index 2, offset 0, threshold 1.0. Measured on the shop window's lamp pole at the dusk spawn of free roam (RTX 5080, D3D12, 1x, 1280x720, the dots counted by parity in x 150 to 167, y 185 to 254): 61 at (x odd, y even) against 9 to 18 at each of the other three; with alpha to mask off (a development switch) 8; with the offsets forced to 2, 2, 2, 2 (no dithering) 8; with a tolerance of 1/2048, 1/512, 1/128 on the thresholds 8, 5, 7. On Vulkan 52 at (x even, y odd) against 7 to 17 (the vinyl work's pictures, same GPU). The footage of the same cutscene from an Xbox 360 (a 12 Mbps 4K upscale of 720p, `launcher/dev/dither-2026-10-27.md`) has the shelter's posts solid against the bright sign behind them.

`alpha_to_mask_tolerance` (a double, 0.00195 by default, restart-only: it is in the translated shader) is subtracted from every coverage threshold in both translators, which is the comparison of alpha rounded to 8 bits (a step of 8-bit alpha is 0.0039, so an alpha under 1.0 by less than half of one counts as 1.0, and every other threshold moves by the same amount, 0.002 at most). `--alpha_to_mask_tolerance=0` compares the exact float as before. The translated shaders have the same instructions with another constant, so there is nothing to measure in time. The shaders do change, so the first start after the patch compiles the pipelines that use alpha to mask again, as after any change of the translators (the launcher's *Prepare shaders* does it before Play).

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| The `alpha_to_mask_tolerance` option | `include/rex/graphics/flags.h`, `src/graphics/flags.cpp` | D3D12, Vulkan (PC, PS5) |
| The thresholds lowered by it | `dxbc_translator_om.cpp` (`CompletePixelShader_AlphaToMaskSample`, also the ROV path), `spirv_translator_rb.cpp` (under `src/graphics/pipeline/shader`; the host render target path and the fragment shader interlock path) | D3D12; Vulkan (PC, PS5) |

The genuine dithering stays: foliage, the distant trees' billboards and the edges of bushes, whose alpha is a fraction, are covered as before (the bush at the window in the pictures). Nothing was measured on the PS5 (the same SDK code, untested there) or on Linux.

## rexglue-resolve-readback-rules.patch

**Why.** Custom vinyls (the layers made in the vinyl editor) show only in the editor and disappear when the player leaves it (issue #11; the same in Xenia, and in Xenia Canary the setting `readback_resolve` fixes it). The likely reason is that the game reads a render-to-texture result on the CPU (Canary's compatibility list labels this game `gpu-readback`, "GPU-written data read on CPU"), and the emulation keeps resolved data on the GPU unless `readback_resolve` copies it back into guest memory. That setting (`none`, `fast`, `some`, `full`) applies to every resolve of every frame, and `full` waits for the GPU after each, so it is too slow to leave on for this one use.

**What it adds.** A way to read back only the resolves that matter, with the GPU and the CPU untouched for the rest:
- `readback_resolve_rules` (string, default empty = off; changes during play): with `readback_resolve` off or on, a resolve that matches a rule is read back in the rule's mode (`full`, immediate and exact, unless the rule says `mode=fast` or `mode=some`). Rules are separated by `;`, a rule is `key=value` pairs separated by `,`, all of which must hold: `addr=LO-HI` (the memory the resolve wrote touches that range of guest physical addresses), `len=LO-HI` (bytes written), `pitch=` and `height=` (the destination's, in pixels), `fmt=N` (its colour format), `src=N` (the copy's source: colour target 0 to 3, depth is 4), `phase=N` (the title's last render phase marker, which needs the title's markers of every phase), `mode=`. A range is `N`, `LO-HI`, `LO-` or `-HI`, inclusive, in decimal or `0x` hex. With the setting empty and `readback_resolve=none`, a resolve takes the code it always took.
- `resolve_log_frames` (development, default 0): `set resolve_log_frames 3` logs every resolve of the next three frames (address, length, destination pitch, height and format, source, render phase, the mode used and the rule that matched), to find what to put in a rule. A frame is the title's own (its frame marker) when it writes them, else a swap command (this title has about 25 swap commands per drawn frame, so the first version, which counted those, logged a millisecond).
- `resolve_log_new` (development, default off): logs each distinct resolve (address, length, pitch, height, format, source, phase) once, the first time it happens after the setting is turned on, with `[new]` at the end of the line: what runs once (a texture built when a car loads) stands out from the ones every frame repeats.
- Both backends (D3D12 and Vulkan) take the mode of a resolve from the same function (`CommandProcessor::GetReadbackResolveModeFor`); the guest markers' frame and phase are kept for it.

**Status.** Built and run on D3D12 and Vulkan (2026-10-27, RTX 5080, the dev pad): the rules parse and an invalid one is left out with a warning, a rule that reads back every resolve of 1 MB or more ran without a fault, the log lists a frame's resolves, and a rule restores the vinyls of the arcade race's opponents (below). The window of the first version of `resolve_log_frames` counted swap commands (about 25 per frame) and was too short; it counts the title's frames now, and `resolve_log_new` finds what runs once.

**Checked** (D3D12 and Vulkan, RTX 5080, the arcade race *Sunset and Vine* with 7 opponents at noon, `launcher/dev/vinyls-2026-10-27.md`). A baseline of 15,707 resolves of the city and the options screen (29 classes by length, pitch, height, format and source) against 115,275 of the race's loading, intro and start (36 classes): the race adds a 512x512 `8_8_8_8` target at `0x050A0000` (1 MB) with its whole mip chain (256x256 at `0x051A0000` down to 32x4 at `0x051F7000`), resolved 113 times in the UI phase (`0x20000000`) within guest frames 2523 to 2810, which nothing in the baseline has. With `readback_resolve=none` the green opponent is plain; with `len=1048576,pitch=512,height=512,fmt=6` (the base level), with `addr=0x050A0000-0x051F7FFF` (the chain) and with `readback_resolve=full` it carries its white and green livery. `len=200704,pitch=224,height=220,fmt=6` (the next new class) restores nothing. On Vulkan the same: none plain, the chain by address and the game's rule restore. The race loads in 5.3 s with the rule and without it. The game's default rule (`mcla_vinyl_readback`) is `len=1048576,pitch=512,height=512,fmt=6;addr=0x051A0000-0x051F7FFF`.

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| The rules, the log, the per-resolve mode, the marker state | `include/rex/graphics/command_processor.h`, `include/rex/graphics/flags.h`, `src/graphics/command_processor.cpp` | D3D12, Vulkan |
| IssueCopy takes the readback path when rules or the log are there to apply; the mode of the resolve | `src/graphics/d3d12/command_processor.cpp`, `src/graphics/vulkan/command_processor.cpp` | D3D12; Vulkan |

## rexglue-resolve-unscaled-copy.patch

**Why.** The rain of Midnight Club: Los Angeles is not drawn at `resolution_scale` above 1 (fork issue #17). The game simulates the rain's 16,384 drops on the GPU, in four 128x128 `k_32_32_FLOAT` render targets that it resolves every frame to four 128 KB textures, and the rain's vertex shader fetches the same memory as vertex data (render to vertex buffer). At 1x a resolve writes the shared memory, which a vertex fetch reads. With resolution scaling a resolve writes only the scaled resolve buffer, which the GPU reads as a texture, and marks the pages of the shared memory as written by the GPU (valid) without writing them, so the vertex fetch sees zeros or the start-up data. A trace on D3D12 (RTX 5080, 2026-10-28; `launcher/dev/rain-unscaled-copy-2026-10-28.md`) measured it: the rain's draw rasterises about 520,000 pixels at 1x and none at 2x or 3x, and the shared memory holds zeros there. Reading the resolves back to guest memory (`readback_resolve_rules`) did not help, because the data a vertex fetch reads is the shared memory. xenia-canary has no GPU-side route either: its `readback_resolve` is the same CPU readback, and its `draw_resolution_scale_threshold` keeps small render targets at 1x (render targets of two scale classes), which this SDK does not have.

**What it adds.**
- `resolve_unscaled_copy_rules` (string, default empty = off; changes during play): the rules of `readback_resolve_rules` (`addr`, `len`, `pitch`, `height`, `fmt`, `src`, `phase`; `mode` is not used). With draw resolution scaling, a resolve that matches a rule is, after the resolve itself, downscaled to the 1x layout by the pass the readback uses (`resolve_downscale`: one host pixel of each block of the scaled data) and written straight into the destination range of the shared memory: on D3D12 the shader's destination is a raw UAV of the shared memory buffer, on Vulkan a storage buffer descriptor of it. No readback buffer, no wait, no CPU access. The texture cache is not touched: the pages stay marked as scaled resolves, so a texture of that memory is still loaded from the scaled buffer.
- `SharedMemory::EnsureRangeAllocated` (public): makes sure the host memory behind a range exists. `RequestRange` returns early when every page is valid (`rexglue-shared-memory-request-fast-path.patch`), which a range marked by a scaled resolve is, whether or not its memory was ever allocated.
- A resolve that does not fit is left as it was, with the reason logged (`unscaled resolve copy: ... -> not done: ...`): not whole 32x32 tiles, not on a 128-byte boundary, not 8, 16, 32 or 64 bits a pixel, not inside the scaled range the resolve made current. The first 16 copies, every 1024th and (with `resolve_log_frames`) all of them are logged.
- `IssueCopy` takes its fast path (the resolve and nothing else) when the option is empty, as before; the rules are matched by the same function as the readback's (`FindMatchingReadbackRule`).
- Both backends. At native resolution the copy is not reached.

**Status.** Written from the SDK's code and the trace; compiled by CI (D3D12 and Vulkan sources); not run when this was written (the chain `rainfix2` runs it). The downscale takes one host pixel of each block (as the readback does; `readback_resolve_half_pixel_offset` moves it to the block's centre); its layout assumption is exact for 8 bytes a pixel at 2x2 (the rain's resolves), reads a pixel of the right block at 3x3, and reads a neighbour's pixel for half or more of the pixels at 4 bytes or less (checked by arithmetic against the scaled addressing of the SDK's shaders: `launcher/dev/scaled-resolve-layout-check.py`, `launcher/dev/rain-unscaled-copy-2026-10-28.md`; that also applies to the vinyl readback at 2x and 3x). Nothing ran on Linux or the PS5.

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| The option, the rules, the match, the log | `include/rex/graphics/command_processor.h`, `include/rex/graphics/flags.h`, `src/graphics/command_processor.cpp` | D3D12, Vulkan |
| `SharedMemory::EnsureRangeAllocated` | `include/rex/graphics/shared_memory.h` | D3D12, Vulkan |
| The downscale into the shared memory after a matching scaled resolve; `IssueCopy` takes the longer path when there are rules | `src/graphics/d3d12/command_processor.cpp`, `src/graphics/vulkan/command_processor.cpp` and their headers | D3D12; Vulkan |

## rexglue-upstream-storage-flush.patch

**Why.** Two faults in how the shader and pipeline storage (the `.xsh` and `.xpso` files under the cache root) reach the disk.
- **A flush that waited for the next request.** `StorageWriteThread` (the D3D12 and the Vulkan pipeline cache each have one) moves a pending flush request into a local flag and runs the `fflush` at the top of its next pass. When both queues were empty it waited first, so what it had written in the last burst before it went idle stayed in the C library's buffer until the next storage request. A game that is killed or crashes then loses its last pipelines (a clean exit closes the files and loses nothing).
- **`"a+"` streams that start at the end of the file.** The storage opens its four files with `"a+b"` and reads the header at once. glibc and Windows' C runtime start an `"a+"` stream at offset 0; BSD libc starts it at the end. There `fread` returned 0, the header check failed and the loader emptied the file and wrote a new header, so nothing was ever kept (upstream saw it on macOS: a 44,430-byte Vulkan storage file came back as its 12-byte header at the next start). The PS5's libc is FreeBSD's, so on the console this is probably happening now, and hidden by the shader pack import (the pack is added again at every start); not observed, as nothing here runs on the console.

**What it changes.**
- `rex::filesystem::OpenFile` (the POSIX one: Linux and the PS5) rewinds a stream opened with both `a` and `+`. Writes on an append stream go to the end whatever the position, so only where reads start changes. Windows has its own `OpenFile` and is not touched.
- The condition that makes the writer thread wait also requires that no flush is pending (`!flush_shaders && !flush_pipelines`), so the thread goes round once more and flushes first. In the Vulkan pipeline cache the fork's `rexglue-vulkan-pipeline-cache-file.patch` had already added `!save_vk_pipeline_cache` to the same condition, which the pull request's hunk (written for the SDK without it) does not apply to; the two are merged by hand.

**From upstream.** Pull request [#529](https://github.com/rexglue/rexglue-sdk/pull/529) to rexglue-sdk, "fix(graphics): keep shader and pipeline storage across runs", by wolfiesch (Wolfie), open when this was taken (2026-10-25), not merged. The code is the pull request's, apart from the merge described above; it is not this fork's work. Licence: BSD 3-Clause, the SDK's own (`LICENSE` of rexglue-sdk, repeated in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`).

**Checked** (Windows, RTX 5080 and Ryzen 7 9800X3D, native resolution, the dev pad; the shader pack's import off with `--mcla_shader_pack_auto=false`, so that the segment is cold and the storage starts empty, a new cache folder for each run). The segment is the game's start, the title, the map zoom-out and the arrival in the city (`boot-to-roam.pad`'s first part), then 5 seconds with nothing happening. Both builds are llvm-mingw builds, origin/main's and this branch's (the update rehearsal's builds before and after).
- *Closed cleanly*, the storage file's size 5 seconds after the segment against its size after the game had closed by itself. Without the patch the file grew at exit, by what the writer thread had written and not flushed: 204 bytes on Vulkan (28,368 to 28,572) and 216 bytes on D3D12 (36,372 to 36,588), three pipelines' records each. With the patch it did not grow: 22,588 bytes on Vulkan and 37,956 on D3D12, the same 5 seconds after the segment and after the exit.
- *Killed* (`taskkill /F`) 5 seconds after the segment, then started again on what it left. Without the patch the Vulkan run had created 358 pipelines and the next start loaded 355 of them (D3D12: 506 and 503), the same three as in the clean close. With the patch the Vulkan run created 329 and the next start loaded 329. D3D12 loaded 510 after 500 counted: its creation count comes from a debug log line that does not see every creation, so the clean-close sizes above are the exact measure there.
- One run for each build, API and way of ending the game. The same build lost the same three pipelines in its kill run and its clean-close run. The first try of this test, with the shader pack's import on for the new build and not for the baseline, was not comparable and is not used.
- The `"a+"` rewind is POSIX only (Windows has its own `OpenFile`, and its C runtime starts such streams at the beginning). The function compiles (a copy of it was built and syntax-checked on its own); nothing here ran on Linux or on the PS5, so the console's truncation at every start is the pull request's reasoning, not something seen.

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| `"a+"` streams rewound | `src/core/filesystem_posix.cpp` | Linux, the PS5 (and macOS) |
| The writer thread flushes before it waits | `src/graphics/d3d12/pipeline_cache.cpp`, `src/graphics/vulkan/pipeline_cache.cpp` | D3D12, Vulkan (all platforms) |

## rexglue-upstream-host-fp-state.patch

**Why.** The host threads that run guest code (the audio worker, deferred procedure calls) never called `FPSCRRegister::InitHost()`: only guest threads (`XThread`) do. Their context starts with `fpscr.csr == 0`, and on x86-64 an MXCSR with the exception mask bits clear means every floating-point exception is *unmasked*. Recompiled guest code writes the control word through `storeFromGuest`, `enableFlushMode` and the like, which passed the word on as it was, so the first inexact result in a vector conversion (`vcfsx`) in the audio worker raised `SIGFPE` on Linux (Kameo, upstream issue #381: 4 of 4 runs crashed within 90 seconds) and `STATUS_FLOAT_INEXACT_RESULT` on Windows (the second pull request). This game has not been seen to do it; the patch is insurance against a crash class whose cause is the same in every title.

**What it changes.** `XHostThread::Execute` calls `fpscr.InitHost()` before the thread's function, as `XThread` does; and `FPSCRRegister::setcsr` runs the value through `InitHostExceptions` (the per-architecture polarity: x86 sets the mask bits, ARM clears the enable bits) before writing it, so no later write can unmask them again. The cost is an OR on each control word write. The pull requests' unit tests are not taken.

**From upstream.** Two pull requests to rexglue-sdk, both open when this was taken (2026-10-25) and not merged: [#382](https://github.com/rexglue/rexglue-sdk/pull/382), "fix(ppc): keep host FP exceptions masked in FPSCRRegister::setcsr", by Azkali (The Great Wizard Azkali), and [#459](https://github.com/rexglue/rexglue-sdk/pull/459), "Initialize host FPSCR state before XHostThread callbacks", by WillianPepal (Willian Palmeira). The code is theirs, taken as it is; not this fork's work. Licence: BSD 3-Clause, the SDK's own (`LICENSE` of rexglue-sdk, repeated in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`).

**Checked.** Windows, built with Clang (msvc toolchain on a fresh clone, llvm-mingw as the launcher updates it, and CI's llvm-mingw build). The game ran as before on both APIs (RTX 5080 and Ryzen 7 9800X3D, native resolution, the dev pad): boot to the city, a race of 90 seconds of game time (one run per build and API) and the short drive of `drive-spawn.pad` (three runs after a warm-up per build and API), against origin/main's build, as a timedemo (`--fixed-dt 60`): free roam 6.86 against 6.85 ms (D3D12) and 6.81 against 6.80 ms (Vulkan), the drive 6.88 against 6.84 ms and 6.86 against 6.83 ms, the race 6.83 against 6.83 ms and 6.85 against 6.80 ms; the GPU guard clean in every run and no warning or error in the game's log that the baseline's does not have.

The mechanism, in a small program built against `rex/ppc/context.h` (a check of the header, not of the game): a thread whose MXCSR has the inexact exception unmasked, a `PPCContext` whose `fpscr.csr` is 0, then `enableFlushModeUnconditional()` (what the generated code of `vcfsx` does) and `1.0f / 3.0f`. With the header as it was, the control word becomes 0x8040 (every exception unmasked) and the division raises 0xC000008F (`STATUS_FLOAT_INEXACT_RESULT`); with the patched header it becomes 0x9FC0 and nothing is raised. That is the `setcsr` half. The `XHostThread::Execute` half is one line and is not covered by a test; this game's audio worker has not been seen to fault without it (origin/main's build ran every test here), and no crash in a game build's audio worker was reproduced, nor Linux's `SIGFPE`.

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| `setcsr` keeps the exceptions masked | `include/rex/ppc/context.h` | all platforms (x86-64 and ARM64 hosts) |
| Host threads initialise the host FP state | `src/system/xthread.cpp` | all platforms |

## rexglue-upstream-linux-wine.patch

**Why.** Three small changes for running the SDK under Wine/Proton and on Linux, found by upstream's Linux and Steam Deck users. None changes anything on native Windows.
- **Wine and the guest memory section** (`src/core/memory_win.cpp`). The guest's 4 GB are one section created `SEC_RESERVE` and committed per range. Wine asks the wineserver for the section's committed range on every `VirtualProtect` and `VirtualQuery` of a view of it (`get_committed_size` in `dlls/ntdll/unix/virtual.c`), and the GPU write watches protect guest pages hundreds of times a frame, so each of those was a round trip to the server. A `SEC_COMMIT` section is answered locally. Wine backs both kinds with the same sparse file, and `MEM_COMMIT` on a view of a committed section only sets the protection. The one difference: guest pages nobody committed are readable and writable instead of faulting, as they already are on Linux and macOS, where the views are mapped read-write up front. The pull request measured MCLA (Proton): 14.3 to 11.3 microseconds of CPU per draw and 17 to 23.8 frames a second.
- **The shared memory name** (`src/core/memory_posix.cpp`). Linux creates the guest memory as a named POSIX shared memory object (`/dev/shm`, 4.8 GB for this game) and removed the name only on a clean exit, so a killed or crashed run left it behind. The views are mapped from the descriptor and never opened again by name, so the name is dropped (`shm_unlink`) right after the object is created, and the object lives exactly as long as the process. The PS5 branch (anonymous `SHM_ANON`) is not touched.
- **RenderDoc** (`src/ui/renderdoc_api.cpp`, `src/core/dynlib_*.cpp`). The renderer *loaded* `renderdoc.dll` / `librenderdoc.so` to see whether RenderDoc was attached, and loading it makes RenderDoc hook the graphics API and slow every draw even when no capture is wanted (the pull request measured `librenderdoc.so` at about 27% of the GPU command thread on a Steam Deck). It now only attaches to the library when it is already in the process (RenderDoc injected itself, as when the game is started from RenderDoc): `DynamicLibrary::Attach`, `GetModuleHandleExW` on Windows and `dlopen` with `RTLD_NOLOAD` on POSIX.

**What it changes beyond the pull requests.** The POSIX `Attach` is compiled only where `RTLD_NOLOAD` is defined (glibc, FreeBSD); elsewhere it reports that nothing is loaded, as the PS5 toolchain's `dlfcn.h` was not available to check. The `dynlib_*.cpp` hunks are placed beside the fork's `CheckLoadable` (`rexglue-low-latency.patch`), where the pull request's context no longer applied; the code is the same.

**From upstream.** Three pull requests to rexglue-sdk, open when this was taken (2026-10-25), not merged: [#517](https://github.com/rexglue/rexglue-sdk/pull/517), "perf(memory): commit the guest memory section up front under Wine", and [#514](https://github.com/rexglue/rexglue-sdk/pull/514), "fix(memory): unlink the shared memory name right after creating it", both by MoonFuji (Kadri Mohammed Mouncef); and [#522](https://github.com/rexglue/rexglue-sdk/pull/522), "fix(ui): attach to RenderDoc only when it is already loaded, never load it", by Meesz. The code is theirs; not this fork's work. Licence: BSD 3-Clause, the SDK's own (`LICENSE` of rexglue-sdk, repeated in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`).

**Checked.** Windows only, and only that nothing changed there: the game runs as before (the frame time and log comparison in `rexglue-upstream-host-fp-state.patch`'s paragraph covers the same builds). Under native Windows `wine_get_version` is not in `ntdll.dll`, so `CreateFileMappingHandle` takes no new branch. RenderDoc is not installed on the test PC, so the attach was not exercised: with no RenderDoc, neither the old load nor the new attach finds the library, and the renderer carries on without its API as before. Syntax-checked with the platform forced (llvm-mingw's clang and libc++ with stand-in headers for what mingw lacks of POSIX, `-fsyntax-only`): `dynlib_posix.cpp` and `renderdoc_api.cpp` for Linux and for the PS5, with `RTLD_NOLOAD` defined and without it, 0 errors. The line added to `memory_posix.cpp` is a copy of the `shm_unlink` call beside it; that file does not compile with mingw's headers, so it was not compiled on its own. Not run under Wine, on Linux or on the PS5: the figures in "Why" are the pull requests' own (a Proton run of this game, and a Steam Deck).

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| The guest memory section committed up front under Wine | `src/core/memory_win.cpp` | Windows build under Wine/Proton (native Windows takes no new branch) |
| The shared memory name dropped after creation | `src/core/memory_posix.cpp` | Linux (not the PS5) |
| `DynamicLibrary::Attach`; RenderDoc only attached | `include/rex/platform/dynlib.h`, `src/core/dynlib_win.cpp`, `src/core/dynlib_posix.cpp`, `src/ui/renderdoc_api.cpp` | all platforms |

## rexglue-upstream-small-fixes.patch

**Why.** Two unrelated corrections, both in code this game runs.
- **Profile settings written with no data** (`src/kernel/xam/xam_user.cpp`, `include/rex/system/xam/user_profile.h`). `XamUserWriteProfileSettings` copied a binary setting with `memcpy(bytes.data(), ptr, size)` into an empty vector, whose `data()` is null: undefined behaviour even when the size is 0, and with clang 22 the compiler used the promise that `memcpy`'s pointers are never null to drop `resize()`'s size check, which ended in a `memset` of `SIZE_MAX` bytes after `operator new(0)` and a heap abort at the next `free` (upstream issue #502, Gears of War on Linux). The vector is now made at its size (zero-filled, which also covers a null data pointer) and copied into only when there is something to copy. The same patch gives `UserProfile::Setting` a virtual destructor: settings are owned and replaced through `std::unique_ptr<Setting>`, so without one the derived buffers leaked and the object was freed with the base class's size.
- **The texture fetch's exponent bias on Vulkan** (`src/graphics/pipeline/shader/spirv_translator_fetch.cpp`). The Vulkan (SPIR-V) shader translator applied the result exponent bias of a texture fetch from bits 13 to 18 of fetch constant word 4; the hardware's is word 3 (D3D12's translator already reads word 3). The patch loads word 3 and uses it for the bias (word 4 is still loaded for the LOD bias and the stacked texture filter). A bias of 0, as nearly every texture has, is the same either way. Xenia Canary found the same and fixed it; the pull request's screenshots show Daytona USA's textures right after.

**From upstream.** Two pull requests to rexglue-sdk, open when this was taken (2026-10-25), not merged: [#503](https://github.com/rexglue/rexglue-sdk/pull/503), "fix(xam): heap corruption on zero-size binary profile settings", by JTM-rootstorm (Michael); and [#490](https://github.com/rexglue/rexglue-sdk/pull/490), "Vulkan: Use fetch_constant_word_3 for exponent bias in SPIR-V translator", by synthic (Alex Abbatiello), a port of xenia-canary's commit [32889f5](https://github.com/xenia-canary/xenia-canary/commit/32889f51beafc207ef3c4e73c807d363dfa133c3). The code is theirs; not this fork's work. Licence: BSD 3-Clause, the SDK's own (`LICENSE` of rexglue-sdk, repeated in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`); xenia-canary is BSD 3-Clause too (Copyright (c) 2015, Ben Vanik).

**Checked.** Windows, built with Clang (msvc toolchain on a fresh clone, llvm-mingw as the launcher updates it, and CI's llvm-mingw build); the game ran as before on both APIs, with the frame times and the log comparison given in `rexglue-upstream-host-fp-state.patch`'s paragraph. The zero-size profile setting is what titles such as Gears of War write; nothing here writes one, so that case was not reproduced, and neither was the leak the virtual destructor closes.

For the exponent bias (#490), pictures on Vulkan, from llvm-mingw builds of origin/main and of this branch (`--fixed-dt 60`, native resolution, the dev pad): the city at the arrival, the pause menu, a short drive and a race at three points, and another set with the game's time stopped (`mcla_freeze`) at the arrival. Separate runs do not reach the same game state, even of one build (time of day, traffic and the car's place differ; two runs of origin/main differ by a mean of 7.9 to 17 out of 255 on the arrival and the pause menu), so a picture is compared with one of another run in the same state, found among 5 to 7 runs of each build. In those pairs the two builds' pictures look the same. Mean absolute difference (0 to 255) at the arrival: 1.05, 1.12, 1.14 and 1.92 between the builds, against 0.46 to 0.60 between two runs of one build in the same state; on the pause menu 3.0 and 3.9 between the builds against 1.35 and 1.85 within one. Amplified difference pictures show edge noise, the walking pedestrian and the loading spinner, and no surface changing in brightness, which an exponent bias would do (it scales a texture's colours); averaged over blocks of 16 by 16 pixels the builds differ by 0.3 to 0.4 (0.9 in one pair, with a moving object in the picture) against 0.2 within one build. So no visible change. The builds differ a little more than two runs of one build do, at edges only; the patch can't be switched at run time, so the same state can't be drawn with both versions, and whether that small excess is this patch or a state that matched a little less well is not settled. The Vulkan pipelines the shaders make compile as before (no new warning or error in the game's log), and the Vulkan frame times in `rexglue-upstream-host-fp-state.patch`'s paragraph are the same builds' (equal within noise).

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| Binary profile settings: no `memcpy` into an empty vector; virtual destructor | `src/kernel/xam/xam_user.cpp`, `include/rex/system/xam/user_profile.h` | all platforms |
| Texture fetch exponent bias from fetch constant word 3 | `src/graphics/pipeline/shader/spirv_translator_fetch.cpp` | Vulkan (all platforms) |

## rexglue-mingw.patch

**Why.** Builds the SDK, `rexglue.exe` and the game with llvm-mingw 20250709 (Clang 20.1.8, lld, libc++ and mingw-w64 on Windows' UCRT), so that a PC needs no Microsoft compiler (`scripts\make_pc.ps1 -Toolchain mingw`). Found by building the SDK that way: every change is small, and guarded or without effect for the Microsoft toolchain (checked: the msvc build's `mcla.exe`, `rexruntime.dll`, `rexgpu-xenos.dll` and the code `rexglue codegen` writes are unchanged). A game built this way was compared with the msvc build's (the same Clang version, 62 scripted runs on both APIs, 2026-10-24): the same pictures, saves and behaviour, and frame times within the benchmarks' own spread.

**What it changes.**
- MinGW's DLLs get Windows names, `rexruntime.dll` and `rexgpu-xenos.dll`, not `librexruntime.dll`: the GPU plugin is loaded by the name `rexgpu-<name>.dll`, and a prefix would have failed at the first start, not at build time.
- `rexruntime.dll` exports the symbols named `__imp_*`: the recompiler names a title's kernel imports `__imp__<name>`, which lld's automatic export always leaves out for MinGW (the Microsoft toolchain exports them through CMake's generated `.def`). `cmake/mingw_imp_exports.cmake` lists them from the objects before the link (2,866 for this game), and `--export-all-symbols` keeps the rest.
- The host links with `-municode` (the SDK's entry point is `wWinMain`), and the SDK and the game compile with `-D_WIN32_WINNT=0x0A00` (mingw-w64 assumes Windows 7; the Windows SDK assumes the current Windows, and `frame_timing.cpp` calls newer APIs) and `-fms-extensions` (`__try`/`__except`).
- `std::chrono::clock_cast` and `clock_time_conversion` stand-ins for LLVM's libc++ (`_LIBCPP_VERSION`) while it does not report the complete C++20 `<chrono>` (`__cpp_lib_chrono` 201907L; 20.1 says 201611L), as already for Apple's and the PS5's, which don't have them; a libc++ that has them is left alone.
- `IDXGraphicsAnalysis` (PIX's programmatic capture) declared where the Windows SDK's `DXProgrammableCapture.h` is missing; `__CRT_UUID_DECL` for the three DXC interfaces the SDK asks `__uuidof` of, which DXC's headers only describe for MSVC; `__uuidof` where the code had `_uuidof` (a typo MSVC's mode accepts); `reinterpret_cast` where a `FARPROC` was assigned to a `void*`; no `endian.h` on Windows in `sha256.cpp`; and `shader_storage_progress.h` drops the macro glslang's `Common.h` defines under MinGW's secure API (`snprintf` as `sprintf_s`), which `std::snprintf` can't be spelt with.
- Not in the SDK: the game's own flags for MinGW (`-fms-extensions`, `_WIN32_WINNT`, and no `-fasync-exceptions`, with the reason) are in the fork's `CMakeLists.txt`, after it includes `generated/rexglue.cmake`, and not in the template of that file: `rexglue codegen` writes the file at every build, and with a case in the template a Linux or PS5 build (which does not apply this patch) would leave the tracked file modified. The Clang driver drops `-fasync-exceptions` for a MinGW target (a warning on every file), and passed on to the compiler itself it crashed Clang 23.1.3 in SimplifyCFG on most of the game's host files, so a fault inside a guest `__try` scope is not caught with this toolchain; this game has no such scope (0 of 29,848 functions), and a title that has one needs the msvc toolchain.

**Upstream:** not offered for now (all upstream work is parked); the changes are small and portable, and meant to be.

| Change | Files | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| DLL and import library names without MinGW's `lib` prefix; `_WIN32_WINNT` and `-fms-extensions` | `CMakeLists.txt` | MinGW |
| `__imp_*` exports of `rexruntime.dll` | `cmake/mingw_imp_exports.cmake` (new), `src/system/CMakeLists.txt` | MinGW |
| `-municode` for the host | `cmake/rexglue_helpers.cmake` | MinGW |
| `clock_cast` stand-ins for LLVM's libc++ without the full C++20 `<chrono>` | `include/rex/chrono/chrono.h` | LLVM's libc++ (llvm-mingw; elsewhere the condition is as it was) |
| PIX capture interface, DXC interface GUIDs | `include/rex/ui/d3d12/d3d12_api.h` | MinGW (the declarations are used where the header is missing) |
| `__uuidof`; `reinterpret_cast` of `FARPROC`; no `endian.h` on Windows | `src/ui/d3d12/d3d12_provider.cpp`, `src/input/xinput/xinput_input_driver.cpp`, `thirdparty/crypto/sha256.cpp` | Windows, all toolchains (the same code) |
| glslang's `snprintf` macro undone | `src/graphics/shader_storage_progress.h` | MinGW (the macro is not defined elsewhere) |

## rexglue-ffmpeg-mingw-config.patch

FFmpeg's Windows configuration is the Microsoft toolchain's (`--toolchain=msvc`): it asks for `crtversion.h`, which mingw-w64 with the UCRT doesn't have (151 files failed). Under `__MINGW32__`, `config.h` sets `HAVE_LIBC_MSVCRT` to 0, which also gives the C99 `printf` specifiers. Applied in the `thirdparty/FFmpeg` submodule, like `rexglue-ffmpeg-ps5-config.patch`; nothing changes for any other compiler.
